.

5
Discussion

In this work we analyze the scaling properties of a wide class of MY-MALA algorithms introduced in
[
33
,
16
]
for smooth targets and for the Laplace distribution.
We show that the scaling properties of MY-MALA are influenced by the relative speed at which the proximal parameter
\lambda_{d}
and the proposal variance
\sigma_{d}
decay to 0 as
d\to\infty
and suggest practical ways to choose
\lambda_{d}
as a function of
\sigma_{d}
to guarantee good results.

In the case of smooth targets, we provide a detailed comparison between MY-MALA and MALA, showing that MY-MALA scales no better than MALA (Theorem
1
). In particular, Theorem
1
–
(a)
shows that if
\lambda_{d}
is too large w.r.t.
\sigma_{d}
then the efficiency of MY-MALA is of order
\mathcal{O}(d^{-1/2})
and therefore worse than the
\mathcal{O}(d^{-1/3})
of MALA, suggesting that
\lambda_{d}
should be chosen to decay approximately as
\sigma_{d}
, if possible.
If
\lambda_{d}
decays sufficiently fast, then MALA and MY-MALA have similar scaling properties and, in the case in which the proximity map is cheaper to compute that the gradient, one can build MY-MALA algorithms which are as efficient as MALA in terms of scaling but more computationally efficient.

In the case of the Laplace distribution, we show that the scaling of MY-MALA is
\mathcal{O}(d^{-2/3})
for any
\lambda_{d}
decaying sufficiently fast w.r.t.
\sigma_{d}
and, in the case
\lambda_{d}=0
, we obtain a novel optimal scaling result for sG-MALA on Laplace targets.

As discussed in Section
4
, our analysis provides some guidance on the choice of the parameters that need to be specified to implement MY-MALA, but this analysis should be complemented by an exploration of the ergodicity properties of MY-MALA to obtain a comprehensive description of the algorithms.
We conjecture that for sufficiently large values of
\lambda
, MY-MALA applied to light tail distributions will be exponentially ergodic; establishing exactly how large should
\lambda
be to guarantee fast convergence is an interesting question that we leave for future work.
Obtaining these results would open the doors to adaptive tuning strategies for MY-MALA, which are likely to produce better results than those given by the strategies currently used.

We assumed throughout this work that we have access to exact evaluations of the proximity map
\prox_{G}^{\lambda}
; while this is true for many functions of interest (e.g.
\ell^{p}
-norms
[
32
]
), in general the optimization problem (
5
) which defines the proximity map needs to be solved approximately. This introduces an
approximation error which could be studied using, e.g., similar tools to those employed in
[
43
]
.

The set up under which we carried out our analysis closely resembles that of
[
37
]
; we anticipate that
2
could be relaxed following similar ideas as those in
[
12
,
24
]
and that our analysis could be extended to
d
-dimensional targets
\pi_{d}
possessing some dependence structure following the approach of
[
44
,
6
,
48
]
.
Finally, the analysis carried out for the Laplace distribution could be extended to other piecewise smooth distributions provided that the moments necessary for the proof in Appendix C can be computed.

Appendix A
Proof of Theorem
1

The proof of Theorem
1
follows that of
[
37
, Theorem 1,
Theorem 2]
and consists of four propositions showing
convergence of the log-acceptance probability to a normal random variable and
(weak) convergence of the process (
15
) to a Langevin diffusion.

We start by recalling and defining a number of quantities that we will use in the following proofs.
Recall that
\sigma_{d}=\ell/d^{\alpha}
, that
\lambda_{d}=\sigma_{d}^{2v}r/2
where
v\geq 1/2
and
r>0

are to be chosen according to the different cases in
Theorem

1
.
Recalling the expression of the proposal given in (
12
) and using the
simplification given in (
13
), we define
the proposal with starting point
\bm{x}^{d}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\bm{y}^{d}(\bm{x}^{d},\bm{z}^{d})=\bm{x}^{d}-\frac{\sigma_{d}^{2}}{2}\nabla G\left(\prox_{G}^{\sigma_{d}^{2v}r/2}(\bm{x}^{d})\right)+\sigma_{d}\bm{z}^{d},

(38)

where
\bm{z}^{d}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Since
G(\bm{x}^{d})=\sum_{i=1}^{d}g(x_{i}^{d})
,
the
i
-th component of the proposal only depends on the
i
-th components of

\bm{x}^{d}
and
\bm{z}^{d}
. Thus, for any
x,z\in\mathbb{R}
we denote

y_{d}(x,z)=x-\frac{\sigma_{d}^{2}}{2}g^{\prime}\left(\prox_{g}^{\sigma_{d}^{2v}r/2}(x)\right)+\sigma_{d}z.

(39)

The proposal for the
chain
(X^{d}_{k})_{k\geq 0}
is then given by
Y^{d}_{k}=\bm{y}^{d}(X^{d}_{k},Z^{d}_{k+1})=(y_{d}(X^{d}_{k,i},Z^{d}_{k+1,i}))_{i\in\{1,\dots,d\}}
.
Let us define the generator of the discrete process
(X_{k}^{d})_{k\geq 0}
for all

V\in\mathrm{C}_{\mathrm{c}}^{\infty}(\real^{d},\real)
, i.e., infinitely differentiable
-valued multivariate functions with compact support, and any
\bm{x}^{d}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{L}_{d}V(\bm{x}^{d})

\displaystyle=d^{2\alpha}\mathbb{E}\left[\left[V(\bm{y}^{d}(\bm{x}^{d},Z^{d}_{1}))-V(\bm{x}^{d})\right]\frac{\pi_{d}(\bm{y}^{d}(\bm{x}^{d},Z^{d}_{1}))q_{d}(\bm{y}^{d}(\bm{x}^{d},Z^{d}_{1}),\bm{x}^{d})}{\pi_{d}(\bm{x}^{d})q_{d}(\bm{x}^{d},\bm{y}^{d}(\bm{x}^{d},Z^{d}_{1}))}\wedge 1\right]

(40)

\displaystyle=d^{2\alpha}\mathbb{E}\left[\left[V(\bm{y}^{d}(\bm{x}^{d},Z^{d}_{1}))-V(\bm{x}^{d})\right]\prod_{i=1}^{d}\exp\left(\phi_{d}(x_{i}^{d},Z^{d}_{1,i})\right)\wedge 1\right],

(41)

where the expectation is w.r.t.
Z^{d}_{1}=(Z^{d}_{1,i})_{i\in\{1,\dots,d\}}
, a

d
-dimensional standard normal random variable, and where we defined

\displaystyle\phi_{d}(x,z)

\displaystyle=\log\frac{\pi(y_{d}(x,z))q(y_{d}(x,z),x)}{\pi(x)q(x,y_{d}(x,z))}

(42)

\displaystyle=g(x)-g(y_{d}(x,z))+\log q(y_{d}(x,z),x)-\log q(x,y_{d}(x,z)).

(43)

In the remainder we will work with one-dimensional functions
V\in\mathrm{C}_{\mathrm{c}}^{\infty}(\real,\real)
applied to the first component of
\bm{x}^{d}
so that

\displaystyle\mathrm{L}_{d}V(\bm{x}^{d})

\displaystyle=d^{2\alpha}\mathbb{E}\left[\left[V(y_{d}(x_{1}^{d},Z^{d}_{1,1}))-V(x_{1}^{d})\right]\frac{\pi_{d}(\bm{y}^{d}(\bm{x}^{d},Z^{d}_{1}))q_{d}(\bm{y}^{d}(\bm{x}^{d},Z^{d}_{1}),\bm{x}^{d})}{\pi_{d}(\bm{x}^{d})q_{d}(\bm{x}^{d},\bm{y}^{d}(\bm{x}^{d},Z^{d}_{1}))}\wedge 1\right]

(44)

\displaystyle=d^{2\alpha}\mathbb{E}\left[\left[V(y_{d}(x_{1}^{d},Z^{d}_{1,1}))-V(x_{1}^{d})\right]\prod_{i=1}^{d}\exp\left(\phi_{d}\left(x_{i}^{d},Z^{d}_{1,i}\right)\right)\wedge 1\right].

(45)

We also define
\widetilde{\mathrm{L}}_{d}
to be a variant of
\mathrm{L}_{d}
in which the first component of the acceptance ratio is omitted:

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathrm{L}}_{d}V(\bm{x}^{d})=d^{2\alpha}\mathbb{E}\left[\left[V(y_{d}(x_{1}^{d},Z^{d}_{1,1}))-V(x_{1}^{d})\right]\prod_{i=2}^{d}\exp\left(\phi_{d}\left(x_{i}^{d},Z^{d}_{1,i}\right)\right)\wedge 1\right].

(46)

We further define the generator of the Langevin diffusion

\displaystyle\mathrm{L}V(x)=\frac{h(\ell,r)}{2}\left[V^{\prime\prime}(x)-g^{\prime}(x)V^{\prime}(x)\right],

(47)

where
h(\ell,r)=\ell^{2}a(\ell,r)
is the speed of the diffusion and

a(\ell,r)=\lim_{d\to\infty}a_{d}(\ell,r)
is given in
Theorem
1
.

We will make use of the derivatives of
g
in (
10
) up to order 8, which we denote by
g^{\prime},g^{\prime\prime},g^{\prime\prime\prime}
and
g^{(k)}
for all
k=4,\dots,8
.
We recall that
(g^{\lambda})^{\prime}
is Lipschitz continuous with Lipschitz constant

\lambda^{-1}

[
41
, Proposition 12.19]
and that
(g^{\lambda})^{\prime}(x)=\lambda^{-1}(\prox_{g}^{\lambda}(x)-x)
, hence

\prox_{g}^{\lambda}
is Lipschitz continuous with Lipschitz constant
1
.

A.1
Identifying the scaling regimes

The scaling regimes are identified following the approach of
[
37
]
: we approximate
\phi_{d}(x,z)
with a Taylor expansion about
\sigma_{d}\to 0
and find for which values of
d
this expansion well approximates
\phi_{d}(x,z)
.

We start by decomposing
\phi_{d}(x,z)=R_{1}(x,z,\sigma_{d})+R_{2}(x,z,\sigma_{d})
, where

\displaystyle R_{1}(x,z,\sigma)

\displaystyle=-g\left[x-\frac{\sigma^{2}}{2}g^{\prime}\left(\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)\right)+\sigma z\right]+g(x),

(48)

\displaystyle R_{2}(x,z,\sigma)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}z^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\left(z-\frac{\sigma}{2}g^{\prime}\left(\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}\left[x+\sigma z-\frac{\sigma^{2}}{2}g^{\prime}\left[\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)\right]\right]\right)\right.

(49)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\left.-\frac{\sigma}{2}g^{\prime}\left[\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)\right]\right)^{2}.

(50)

We then compute the derivatives of
R_{1},R_{2}
w.r.t.
\sigma
at
\sigma=0
as shown in Appendix
B.2
. This shows that

\displaystyle R_{1}(x,z,0)+R_{2}(x,z,0)

\displaystyle=0,

(51)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial R_{1}}{\partial\sigma}(x,z,\sigma)_{\mid\sigma=0}+\frac{\partial R_{2}}{\partial\sigma}(x,z,\sigma)_{\mid\sigma=0}

\displaystyle=0,

(52)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{2}R_{1}}{\partial\sigma^{2}}(x,z,\sigma)_{\mid\sigma=0}+\frac{\partial^{2}R_{2}}{\partial\sigma^{2}}(x,z,\sigma)_{\mid\sigma=0}

\displaystyle=2zg^{\prime\prime}(x)\frac{\partial}{\partial\sigma}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}.

(53)

Looking at the derivatives of the proximity map w.r.t.
\sigma
in Appendix
B.3
we find that for
v=1/2
we have

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{2}R_{1}}{\partial\sigma^{2}}(x,z,\sigma)_{\mid\sigma=0}+\frac{\partial^{2}R_{2}}{\partial\sigma^{2}}(x,z,\sigma)_{\mid\sigma=0}

\displaystyle=-rzg^{\prime\prime}(x)g^{\prime}(x).

(54)

while for
v>1/2
we have

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{2}R_{1}}{\partial\sigma^{2}}(x,z,\sigma)_{\mid\sigma=0}+\frac{\partial^{2}R_{2}}{\partial\sigma^{2}}(x,z,\sigma)_{\mid\sigma=0}

\displaystyle=0.

(55)

Proceeding similarly for higher order derivatives we identify the Taylor expansions in Proposition
3
,
7
and
11
below.

A.2
Auxiliary results for the proof of case
(a)

First, we characterize the limit behaviour of the acceptance ratio (
16
).

Proposition 3
.

Under
0
,
1
and
2
, if
\alpha=1/4
,
\beta=1/8
and
r>0
, then

(i)

the log-acceptance ratio (
42
), when
d\to\infty
, satisfies the following Taylor expansion

\displaystyle\phi_{d}(x,z)

\displaystyle=d^{-1/2}C_{2}(x,z)+d^{-3/4}C_{3}(x,z)+d^{-1}C_{4}(x,z)+C_{5}(x,z,\sigma_{d}),

(56)

where
C_{2}(x,z)
is given in (
63
),
C_{3}
and
C_{4}
are
polynomials in
z
and the derivatives of
g
, such that

\mathbb{E}[C_{3}(X^{d}_{0,1},Z^{d}_{1,1})]=0
and

\mathbb{E}[C_{2}(X^{d}_{0,1},Z^{d}_{1,1})^{2}]=-2\mathbb{E}[C_{4}(X^{d}_{0,1},Z^{d}_{1,1})]
;

(ii)

there exists sets
F_{d}\subseteq\real^{d}
with
d^{2\alpha}\pi_{d}(F_{d}^{c})\to 0
such that

\displaystyle\lim_{d\to\infty}\sup_{\bm{x}^{d}\in F_{d}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lvert\sum_{i=2}^{d}\phi_{d}(x_{i}^{d},Z^{d}_{1,i})-d^{-1/2}\sum_{i=2}^{d}C_{2}(x_{i}^{d},Z^{d}_{1,i})+\frac{\ell^{4}K_{1}(r)^{2}}{2}\right\rvert\right]=0,

(57)

where
K_{1}(r)
is given in
Theorem

1
–
(a)
.

Proof.

Take one component of the log-acceptance ratio

\displaystyle\phi_{d}(x,z)=g(x)-g(y_{d}(x,z))+\log q(y_{d}(x,z),x)-\log q(x,y_{d}(x,z)),

(58)

with
y_{d}(x,z)=x-\sigma_{d}^{2}g^{\prime}(\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}_{d}r/2}(x))/2+\sigma_{d}z
. We have that
\phi_{d}(x,z)=R_{1}(x,z,\sigma_{d})+R_{2}(x,z,\sigma_{d})
, where

\displaystyle R_{1}(x,z,\sigma)

\displaystyle=-g\left[x-\frac{\sigma^{2}}{2}g^{\prime}\left(\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)\right)+\sigma z\right]+g(x),

(59)

\displaystyle R_{2}(x,z,\sigma)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}z^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\left(z-\frac{\sigma}{2}g^{\prime}\left(\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}\left[x+\sigma z-\frac{\sigma^{2}}{2}g^{\prime}\left[\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)\right]\right]\right)\right.

(60)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\left.-\frac{\sigma}{2}g^{\prime}\left[\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)\right]\right)^{2}.

(61)

Following the approach of
[
37
]
we approximate
\phi_{d}(x,z)
with a Taylor expansion about
\sigma_{d}\to 0
.

(i)

Using a Taylor expansion of order 5, we obtain

\displaystyle\phi_{d}(x,z)

\displaystyle=d^{-1/2}C_{2}(x,z)+d^{-3/4}C_{3}(x,z)+d^{-1}C_{4}(x,z)+C_{5}(x,z,\sigma_{d}),

(62)

where

\displaystyle C_{2}(x,z)

\displaystyle=\frac{\ell^{2}}{2}\left(-rzg^{\prime\prime}(x)g^{\prime}(x)\right),

(63)

C_{3}(x,z)
and
C_{4}(x,z)
are given in
Section

B.1.1
and we use the integral form for the remainder

\displaystyle C_{5}(x,z,\sigma_{d})=\int_{0}^{\sigma_{d}}\left.\frac{\partial^{5}}{\partial\sigma^{5}}R(x,z,\sigma)\right|_{\sigma=u}\frac{(\sigma_{d}-u)^{4}}{4!}\mathrm{d}u,

(64)

with
u
between 0 and
\sigma_{d}
and the derivatives of
R_{1}
and
R_{2}
given in Appendix
B.2
.
In addition, integrating by parts and using the moments of
Z^{d}_{1,1}
we find that

\mathbb{E}[C_{2}(X^{d}_{0,1},Z^{d}_{1,1})]=\mathbb{E}[C_{3}(X^{d}_{0,1},Z^{d}_{1,1})]=0
and

\displaystyle 2\mathbb{E}\left[C_{4}(X^{d}_{0,1},Z^{d}_{1,1})\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[C_{2}(X^{d}_{0,1},Z^{d}_{1,1})^{2}\right]=0.

(65)

(ii)

To construct the sets
F_{d}
, consider, for
j=3,4
,
F_{d,j}=F_{d,j}^{1}\cap F_{d,j}^{2}
where we define

\displaystyle F_{d,j}^{1}=\left\{\bm{x}^{d}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:\left\lvert\sum_{i=2}^{d}\mathbb{E}\left[C_{j}(x_{i}^{d},Z^{d}_{1,i})-C_{j}(X^{d}_{0,i},Z^{d}_{1,i})\right]\right\rvert\leq d^{5/8}\right\},

(66)

and

\displaystyle F_{d,j}^{2}=\left\{\bm{x}^{d}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:\left\lvert\sum_{i=2}^{d}V_{j}(x_{i}^{d})-\mathbb{E}\left[V_{j}(X^{d}_{0,i})\right]\right\rvert\leq d^{6/5}\right\},

(67)

where
V_{j}(x):=\var(C_{j}(x,Z^{d}_{1,1}))
.
Using Markov’s inequality and the fact that
C_{j},V_{j}
are bounded by
polynomials since
g
and its derivatives are bounded by polynomials, it is
easy to show that
d^{1/2}\pi_{d}((F_{d,j}^{1})^{c})\to 0
and
d^{1/2}\pi_{d}((F_{d,j}^{2})^{c})\to 0
, from which follows
d^{1/2}\pi_{d}(F_{d,j}^{c})\to 0
as
d\to\infty
.
To prove
\mathrm{L}^{1}
convergence of
C_{j}
for
j=3,4
, observe that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\sum_{i=2}^{d}C_{j}(x_{i}^{d},Z^{d}_{1,i})-\mathbb{E}\left[C_{j}(X^{d}_{0,1},Z^{d}_{1,1})\right]\right)^{2}\right]

(68)

\displaystyle\quad=\sum_{i=2}^{d}V_{j}(x_{i}^{d})+\left(\sum_{i=2}^{d}\mathbb{E}\left[C_{j}(x_{i}^{d},Z^{d}_{1,i})-C_{j}(X^{d}_{0,1},Z^{d}_{1,1})\right]\right)^{2},

(69)

and that, for
\bm{x}^{d}\in F_{d,j}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\sum_{i=2}^{d}C_{j}(x_{i}^{d},Z^{d}_{1,i})-\mathbb{E}\left[C_{j}(X^{d}_{0,1},Z^{d}_{1,1})\right]\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[V_{j}(x_{1}^{d})\right](d-1)+d^{6/5}+d^{5/4}.

(70)

Thus, the third and fourth term in the Taylor expansion (
62
) converge in
\mathrm{L}^{1}
to 0 and
-\ell^{4}K_{1}^{2}(r)/2
respectively.
Now, consider
C_{5}(x_{i}^{d},Z^{d}_{1,i},\sigma_{d})
. We can bound
\frac{\partial^{5}R}{\partial\sigma^{5}}(x,z,\sigma)
with the derivatives of
g
evaluated at

\displaystyle x+\frac{\sigma^{2}}{2}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)+\sigma z\qquad\textrm{and}\qquad\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x).

(71)

Under our assumptions, the derivatives of
g
are bounded by polynomials
M_{0}
, it follows that there exist polynomials
p
of the form

A\left(1+\left[\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)\right]^{N}\right)\left(1+z^{N}\right)\left(1+x^{N}\right)\left(1+\sigma^{N}\right),

for sufficiently large
A
and sufficiently large even integer
N
, such that

\displaystyle\left\lvert g^{(k)}\left[x+\frac{\sigma^{2}}{2}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)+\sigma_{d}z\right]\right\rvert\vee\left\lvert g^{(k)}\left[\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)\right]\right\rvert

(72)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\leq p(\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x),x,z,\sigma_{d}).

(73)

In addition,
|\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)|\leq C(1+|x|)
for some
C\geq 1
, and we can bound

\displaystyle p(\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x),x,z,\sigma)\leq A\left(1+z^{N}\right)\left(1+x^{2N}\right)\left(1+\sigma^{N}\right).

(74)

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left|C_{5}(x_{i}^{d},Z^{d}_{1,i},\sigma_{d})\right|\right]

\displaystyle\leq A\mathbb{E}\left[1+(Z^{d}_{1,i})^{N}\right]\left(1+(x_{i}^{d})^{2N}\right)\int_{0}^{\sigma_{d}}(1+u^{N})\frac{(\sigma_{d}-u)^{4}}{4!}\mathrm{d}u

(75)

\displaystyle\leq A\mathbb{E}\left[1+(Z^{d}_{1,i})^{N}\right]\left(1+(x_{i}^{d})^{2N}\right)d^{-5/2}

(76)

\displaystyle\leq A\left(1+(x_{i}^{d})^{2N}\right)d^{-5/2},

(77)

where the last inequality follows since all the moments of
Z^{d}_{1}
are bounded.
Let us denote
p(x)=A\left(1+x^{2N}\right)
and

\displaystyle F_{d,5}=\left\{\bm{x}^{d}\in\real^{d}:\left\lvert d^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{d}p(x_{i}^{d})-\mathbb{E}\left[p(X^{d}_{0,i})\right]\right\rvert<1\right\}.

(78)

By Chebychev’s inequality we have
\pi_{d}(F_{d,5}^{c})\leq\var(p(X^{d}_{0,1}))d^{-1}
. Additionally, for all
\bm{x}^{d}\in F_{d,5}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=2}^{d}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|C_{5}(x_{i}^{d},Z^{d}_{1,i},\sigma_{d})\right|\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=2}^{d}d^{-5/2}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[p(X^{d}_{0,1})\right]+d^{-1}\right)

(79)

\displaystyle\leq d^{-3/2}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[p(X^{d}_{0,1})\right]+1\right).

(80)

Finally, set
F_{d}=\cap_{j=3}^{5}F_{d,j}
. On
F_{d}
the last three terms of (
62
) converge uniformly in
\mathrm{L}^{1}
, and (
57
) follows using the triangle inequality.

∎

Next, we compare the generator
\mathrm{L}_{d}
and
\widetilde{\mathrm{L}}_{d}
in (
45
) and (
46
) respectively.

Proposition 4
.

Under
0
,
1
and
2
, if
\alpha=1/4
,
\beta=1/8
and
r>0
, there exists sets
S_{d}\subseteq\real^{d}
with
d^{2\alpha}\pi_{d}(S_{d}^{c})\to 0
such that for any
V\in\mathrm{C}_{\mathrm{c}}^{\infty}(\real,\real)

\displaystyle\lim_{d\to\infty}\sup_{\bm{x}^{d}\in S_{d}}\left\lvert\mathrm{L}_{d}V(\bm{x}^{d})-\widetilde{\mathrm{L}}_{d}V(\bm{x}^{d})\right\rvert=0,

(81)

and

\displaystyle\lim_{d\to\infty}\sup_{\bm{x}^{d}\in S_{d}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lvert\left(\exp\left(\sum_{i=1}^{d}\phi_{d}(x^{d}_{i},Z^{d}_{1,i})\right)\wedge 1\right)-\left(\exp\left(\sum_{i=2}^{d}\phi_{d}(x^{d}_{i},Z^{d}_{1,i})\right)\wedge 1\right)\right\rvert\right]=0.

(82)

Proof.

The function
x\mapsto\exp(x)\wedge 1
is Lipschitz continuous with Lipschitz constant 1, hence

\displaystyle\left\lvert\mathrm{L}_{d}V(\bm{x}^{d})-\widetilde{\mathrm{L}}_{d}V(\bm{x}^{d})\right\rvert\leq d^{2\alpha}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|V\left(y_{d}(x^{d}_{1},Z^{d}_{1,1})\right)-V(x_{1}^{d})\right||R(x_{1}^{d},Z^{d}_{1,1},\sigma_{d})|\right],

(83)

where
R(x,z,\sigma)=R_{1}(x,z,\sigma)+R_{2}(x,z,\sigma)
as in (
50
).
Using a Taylor expansion of order 1 about
\sigma=0
with integral remainder:

\displaystyle R(x,z,\sigma)=R(x,z,0)+\left.\frac{\partial R}{\partial\sigma}(x,z,\sigma)\right|_{\sigma=0}\sigma+\int_{0}^{\sigma}\left.\frac{\partial^{2}R}{\partial\sigma^{2}}(x,z,\sigma)\right|_{\sigma=u}(\sigma-u)\mathrm{d}u,

(84)

we obtain

\displaystyle R(x,z,\sigma)=\int_{0}^{\sigma}\left.\frac{\partial^{2}R}{\partial\sigma^{2}}(x,z,\sigma)\right|_{\sigma=u}(\sigma-u)\mathrm{d}u,

(85)

where
\frac{\partial^{2}R}{\partial\sigma^{2}}(x,z,\sigma)
is bounded by the derivatives of
g
evaluated at

\displaystyle x+\frac{\sigma^{2}}{2}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)+\sigma z\qquad\textrm{and}\qquad\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x).

(86)

Under our assumptions, the derivatives of
g
are bounded by polynomials
M_{0}
, it follows that there exist polynomials
p
of the form

A\left(1+\left[\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)\right]^{N}\right)\left(1+z^{N}\right)\left(1+x^{N}\right)\left(1+\sigma^{N}\right),

for sufficiently large
A
and sufficiently large even integer
N
, such that

\displaystyle\left\lvert g^{(k)}\left[x+\frac{\sigma^{2}}{2}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)+\sigma z\right]\right\rvert\vee\left\lvert g^{(k)}\left[\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)\right]\right\rvert\leq p(\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x),x,z,\sigma).

(87)

Proceeding as in Proposition
3
, we can bound

\displaystyle p(\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x),x,z,\sigma)\leq A\left(1+z^{N}\right)\left(1+x^{2N}\right)\left(1+\sigma^{N}\right).

(88)

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\left|R\left(x_{1}^{d},Z^{d}_{1,1},\sigma_{d}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq A\left(1+(Z^{d}_{1,1})^{N}\right)\left(1+(x_{1}^{d})^{2N}\right)

(89)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\times\int_{0}^{\sigma_{d}}(1+u^{N})(\sigma_{d}-u)\mathrm{d}u\leq A\left(1+(Z^{d}_{1,1})^{N}\right)\left(1+(x_{1}^{d})^{2N}\right)\frac{\sigma_{d}^{2}}{2}.

(90)

Since
V\in\mathrm{C}_{\mathrm{c}}^{\infty}(\real,\real)
, there exists a constant
C
such that

\displaystyle\left|V\left(y_{d}(x^{d}_{1},Z^{d}_{1,1})\right)-V(x_{1}^{d})\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\left|y_{d}(x^{d}_{1},Z^{d}_{1,1})-x_{1}^{d}\right|

(91)

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\sigma_{d}|Z_{1,1}^{d}|+\frac{\sigma_{d}^{2}}{2}\left|g^{\prime}\left(\prox_{g}^{\sigma_{d}^{2v}r/2}(x_{1}^{d})\right)\right|\right).

(92)

Recalling that
g^{\prime}(\prox_{g}^{\lambda}(x))=(g^{\lambda})^{\prime}(x)
with

(g^{\lambda})^{\prime}
Lipschitz continuous with Lipschitz constant
\lambda^{-1}
,
we have

\displaystyle\left|g^{\prime}\left(\prox_{g}^{\sigma_{d}^{2v}r/2}(x_{1}^{d})\right)\right|\leq\frac{2}{\sigma_{d}^{2v}r}(1+|x_{1}^{d}|),

(93)

and

\displaystyle\left|V\left(y_{d}(x^{d}_{1},Z^{d}_{1,1})\right)-V(x_{1}^{d})\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\sigma_{d}|Z^{d}_{1,1}|+\frac{\sigma_{d}^{2-2m}}{r}\left(1+|x_{1}^{d}|\right)\right)

(94)

\displaystyle\leq C\sigma_{d}\left(|Z^{d}_{1,1}|+\frac{1}{r}\left(1+|x_{1}^{d}|\right)\right),

(95)

since
v=1/2
.
Combining (
90
) and (
94
) we obtain

\displaystyle d^{2\alpha}\left|V\left(y_{d}(x^{d}_{1},Z^{d}_{1,1})\right)-V(x_{1}^{d})\right|\left|R(x_{1}^{d},Z^{d}_{1,1},\sigma_{d})\right|

(96)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\leq C\sigma_{d}\left(1+(Z^{d}_{1,1})^{N}\right)\left(1+(x_{i}^{d})^{2N}\right)\left(|Z^{d}_{1,1}|+\frac{1}{r}(1+|x_{1}^{d}|)\right),

(97)

for some
C>0
.

Set
S_{d}
to be the set in which
1+(x_{1}^{d})^{2N+1}\leq d^{\alpha/2}
, applying Markov’s inequality we obtain

\displaystyle d^{2\alpha}\pi_{d}(S_{d}^{c})=d^{2\alpha}\pi_{d}\left(\left(1+(x_{1}^{d})^{2N+1}\right)^{5}\geq d^{5\alpha/2}\right)\leq d^{-\alpha/2}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(1+(x_{1}^{d})^{2N+1}\right)^{5}\right]\underset{d\to\infty}{\longrightarrow}0.

(98)

Recalling that
|Z^{d}_{1,1}|
and
1+(Z^{d}_{1,1})^{N}
are bounded, we have that

\displaystyle\sup_{\bm{x}^{d}\in S_{d}}\left\lvert\mathrm{L}_{d}V(\bm{x}^{d})-\widetilde{\mathrm{L}}_{d}V(\bm{x}^{d})\right\rvert\leq Cd^{\alpha/2}\frac{\ell}{d^{\alpha}}\underset{d\to\infty}{\longrightarrow}0.

(99)

The second results follows from (
90
) using the same argument.
∎

The following result considers the convergence to the generator of the Langevin diffusion (
47
).

Proposition 5
.

Under
0
,
1
and
2
, if

\alpha=1/4
,
\beta=1/8
and
r>0
, there exists sets
T_{d}\subseteq\real^{d}
with
d^{2\alpha}\pi_{d}(T_{d}^{c})\to 0
as
d\to\infty
, such that for any
V\in\mathrm{C}_{\mathrm{c}}^{\infty}(\real,\real)

\displaystyle\lim_{d\to\infty}\sup_{\bm{x}^{d}\in T_{d}}\left\lvert d^{2\alpha}\mathbb{E}\left[V\left(y_{d}(x^{d}_{1},Z^{d}_{1,1})\right)-V(x_{1}^{d})\right]-\frac{\ell^{2}}{2}(V^{\prime\prime}(x_{1}^{d})+g^{\prime}(x_{1}^{d})V^{\prime}(x_{1}^{d}))\right\rvert=0.

(100)

Proof.

Take

\displaystyle y_{d}(x^{d}_{1},Z^{d}_{1,1})=x_{1}^{d}+\frac{\sigma_{d}^{2}}{2}g^{\prime}\left(\prox_{g}^{\sigma_{d}^{2v}r/2}(x_{1}^{d})\right)+\sigma_{d}Z^{d}_{1,1},

(101)

and use a Taylor expansion of order 2 of

\displaystyle W(x,z,\sigma)=V\left[x+\frac{\sigma^{2}}{2}g^{\prime}\left(\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)\right)+\sigma z\right],

(102)

about
\sigma=0
with integral remainder:

\displaystyle W(x,z,\sigma)

\displaystyle=W(x,z,0)+\left.\frac{\partial W}{\partial\sigma}(x,z,\sigma)\right|_{\sigma=0}\sigma+\frac{1}{2}\left.\frac{\partial^{2}W}{\partial\sigma^{2}}(x,z,\sigma)\right|_{\sigma=0}\sigma^{2}

(103)

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{\sigma}\left.\frac{\partial^{3}W}{\partial\sigma^{3}}(x,z,\sigma)\right|_{\sigma=u}\frac{(\sigma-u)^{2}}{2}\mathrm{d}u.

(104)

Using the derivatives

\displaystyle W(x,z,0)

\displaystyle=V(x),\quad\left.\frac{\partial W}{\partial\sigma}(x,z,\sigma)\right|_{\sigma=0}=V^{\prime}(x)z,

(105)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{2}W}{\partial\sigma^{2}}(x,z,\sigma)

\displaystyle=V^{\prime\prime}(x)z^{2}+V^{\prime}(x)g^{\prime}(x),

(106)

and recalling that
\mathbb{E}\left[Z^{d}_{1,1}\right]=0,\mathbb{E}\left[(Z^{d}_{1,1})^{2}\right]=1
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[V\left(y_{d}(x^{d}_{1},Z^{d}_{1,1})\right)-V(x_{1}^{d})\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{\sigma_{d}^{2}}{2}\left[V^{\prime\prime}(x_{1}^{d})+V^{\prime}(x_{1}^{d})g^{\prime}(x_{1}^{d})\right]

(107)

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{\sigma_{d}}\left.\frac{\partial^{3}W}{\partial\sigma^{3}}(x_{1}^{d},Z^{d}_{1,1},\sigma)\right|_{\sigma=u}\frac{(\sigma_{d}-u)^{2}}{2}\mathrm{d}u\right].

(108)

Proceeding as in the previous proposition, we can bound

\displaystyle\left\lvert\int_{0}^{\sigma_{d}}\frac{\partial^{3}W}{\partial\sigma^{3}}\left.(x_{1}^{d},Z^{d}_{1,1},\sigma)\right|_{\sigma=u}\frac{(\sigma_{d}-u)^{2}}{2}\mathrm{d}u\right\rvert\leq A\left(1+(Z^{d}_{1,1})^{N}\right)\left(1+(x_{i}^{d})^{2N}\right)d^{-3\alpha}.

(109)

Setting
T_{d}
to be the set in which
(1+(x_{1}^{d})^{2N})\leq d^{\alpha/2}
, the
result follows by applying Markov’s inequality as in
Proposition
4
.
∎

Before proceeding to stating and proving the last auxiliary result, let us
denote by
\psi_{1}:\real\to[0,+\infty)
the characteristic function of the
distribution
\mathrm{N}(0,\ell^{4}K_{1}^{2}(r))
, where
K_{1}^{2}(r)
is given
in
Theorem

1
–
(a)
,

\displaystyle\psi_{1}(t)=\exp(-t^{2}\ell^{4}K^{2}_{1}(r)/2),

(110)

and by
\psi_{1}^{d}(\bm{x}^{d};t)=\int\exp(itw)\mathcal{Q}^{d}_{1}(\bm{x}^{d};\mathrm{d}w)
the characteristic functions associated with the law

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{1}^{d}(\bm{x}^{d};\cdot)=\mathcal{L}\left\{d^{-1/2}\sum_{i=2}^{d}C_{2}(x_{i}^{d},Z^{d}_{1,i})\right\}.

(111)

Proposition 6
.

Under
0
,
1
and
2
, if
\alpha=1/4
,
\beta=1/8
and
r>0
, there exists a sequence of sets
H_{d}\subseteq\real^{d}
such that

(i)

\lim_{d\to\infty}d^{2\alpha}\pi_{d}(H_{d}^{c})=0
,

(ii)

for all
t\in\real
,
\lim_{d\to\infty}\sup_{\bm{x}^{d}\in H_{d}}|\psi_{1}^{d}(\bm{x}^{d};t)-\psi_{1}(t)|=0
,

(iii)

for all bounded continuous function
\chi:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
,

\lim_{d\to\infty}\sup_{\bm{x}^{d}\in H_{d}}\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{Q}_{1}^{d}(\bm{x}^{d};\mathrm{d}u)\chi(u)-\left(2\pi\ell^{4}K_{1}^{2}(r)\right)^{-1/2}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\chi(u)e^{-u^{2}/(2\ell^{4}K_{1}^{2}(r))}\mathrm{d}u\right|=0,

(112)

(iv)

in particular,

\displaystyle\lim_{d\to\infty}\sup_{\bm{x}^{d}\in H_{d}}\left|\mathbb{E}\left[1\wedge\exp\left(d^{-1/2}\sum_{i=2}^{d}C_{2}(x_{i}^{d},Z^{d}_{1,i})-\frac{\ell^{4}K_{1}^{2}(r)}{2}\right)\right]-2\Phi\left(-\frac{\ell^{2}K_{1}(r)}{2}\right)\right\rvert=0,

(113)

where
\Phi
is the distribution function of the standard normal random variable.

Proof.

(i)

Define the functions
h_{j}(x)=\left[-g^{\prime\prime}(x)g^{\prime}(x)\right]^{j}
with
j=1,\dots,4
and let
H_{d}=H_{d,1}\cap H_{d,2}
where

\displaystyle H_{d,1}

\displaystyle=\left\{\bm{x}^{d}\in\real^{d}:\left|\frac{1}{d}\sum_{i=2}^{d}h_{j}(x_{i}^{d})-\int_{\mathbb{R}}h_{j}(u)\pi(u)\mathrm{d}u\right\rvert\leq d^{1/3}\textrm{ for }j=1,\dots,4\right\},

(114)

\displaystyle H_{d,2}

\displaystyle=\left\{\bm{x}^{d}\in\real^{d}:|h_{j}(x_{i}^{d})|\leq d^{2/3}\textrm{ for }i=1,\dots,d\textrm{ and }j=1,\dots,4\right\}.

(115)

Using Chebychev’s inequality, the fact that the derivatives of
g
are bounded by polynomials and that
\pi
has finite moments, we have
d^{1/2}\pi_{d}((H_{d,1})^{c})\to 0
as
d\to\infty
. Similarly, by Markov’s inequality we have
d^{1/2}\pi_{d}((H_{d,2})^{c})\to 0
as
d\to\infty
.

(ii)

We follow
[
37
, Lemma 3(b)]
and decompose

\displaystyle|\psi_{1}^{d}(\bm{x}^{d};t)-\psi_{1}(t)|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\psi_{1}^{d}(\bm{x}^{d};t)-\prod_{i=2}^{d}\left(1-\frac{t^{2}}{2d}v(x_{i}^{d})\right)\right\rvert

(116)

\displaystyle+\left|\prod_{i=2}^{d}\left(1-\frac{t^{2}}{2d}v(x_{i}^{d})\right)-\prod_{i=2}^{d}\exp\left(-t^{2}\frac{v(x_{i}^{d})}{2d}\right)\right\rvert

\displaystyle+\left|\prod_{i=2}^{d}\exp\left(-t^{2}\frac{v(x_{i}^{d})}{2d}\right)-\exp\left(-t^{2}\frac{\ell^{4}K_{1}(r)^{2}}{2}\right)\right\rvert,

where
v(x_{i}^{d})=\var(C_{2}(x_{i}^{d},Z^{d}_{1,i}))=\mathbb{E}[C_{2}(x^{d}_{i},Z_{1,i}^{d})^{2}]
, where the expectation is taken w.r.t.
Z^{d}_{1,i}
.
For the first term, decompose the characteristic function
\psi_{1}^{d}(\bm{x}^{d};t)=\prod_{i=2}^{d}\theta_{i}^{d}(x_{i}^{d};t)
as the product of the characteristic functions of
d^{-1/2}W_{i}
where we define
W_{i}=C_{2}(x_{i}^{d},Z^{d}_{1,i})
, using
[
17
, equation (3.3.3)]
as in the proof of
[
17
, Theorem 3.4.10]
we obtain

\displaystyle\left|\theta_{i}^{d}(x_{i}^{d};t)-\left(1-\frac{t^{2}}{2d}v(x_{i}^{d})\right)\right\rvert\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{|t|^{3}}{d^{3/2}}\frac{|W_{i}|^{3}}{3!}\wedge\frac{2|t|^{2}}{d}\frac{|W_{i}|^{2}}{2!}\right]

(117)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{|t|^{3}}{d^{3/2}}\frac{|W_{i}|^{3}}{3!};|W_{i}|\leq d^{1/2}\varepsilon\right]+\frac{t^{2}}{d}\mathbb{E}\left[|W_{i}|^{2};|W_{i}|>d^{1/2}\varepsilon\right]

(118)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{\varepsilon|t|^{3}}{6d}\mathbb{E}\left[|W_{i}|^{2}\right]+\frac{t^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}d^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[|W_{i}|^{4}\right],

(119)

for any
\varepsilon>0
. For sufficiently large
d
, we have that
t^{2}v(x_{i}^{d})/(2d)\leq 1
for
\bm{x}\in H_{d,2}
, and we can use
[
17
, Lemma 3.4.3]

\displaystyle\left|\psi_{j}^{d}(\bm{x}^{d};t)-\prod_{i=2}^{d}\left(1-\frac{t^{2}}{2d}v(x_{i}^{d})\right)\right\rvert\leq\sum_{i=2}^{d}\left(\frac{\varepsilon|t|^{3}}{6d}\mathbb{E}\left[|W_{i}|^{2}\right]+\frac{t^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}d^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[|W_{i}|^{4}\right]\right)

(120)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{\varepsilon\ell^{4}|t|^{3}}{6}(K_{1}^{2}(r)+D_{1}d^{-1/3})+\frac{t^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}d}(\mathbb{E}\left[|W_{i}|^{4}\right]+D_{2}\ell^{8}d^{-1/4}),

(121)

where the last inequality follows from the fact that
\bm{x}^{d}\in H_{d,2}
and
D_{1},D_{2}
are positive constants.
For any
\delta>0
we can chose
\varepsilon
small enough so that the first term in the above is less than
\delta/2
and we can chose
d
sufficiently large to make the second term less than
\delta/2
. Thus, for any
\delta>0
we can find
\varepsilon>0
and
d\in\mathbb{N}
such that

\displaystyle\left|\psi_{1}^{d}(\bm{x}^{d};t)-\prod_{i=2}^{d}\left(1-\frac{t^{2}}{2d}v(x_{i}^{d})\right)\right\rvert<\delta,

(122)

the uniform convergence then follows.
The second term in (
116
) converges to 0 uniformly for all
\bm{x}^{d}\in H_{d,1}
; while
for the third term in (
116
) we use again
[
17
, Lemma 3.4.3]

\displaystyle\left|\prod_{i=2}^{d}\exp\left(-t^{2}\frac{v(x_{i}^{d})}{2d}\right)-\exp\left(-t^{2}\frac{\ell^{4}K_{1}(r)^{2}}{2}\right)\right\rvert\leq\sum_{i=2}^{d}\frac{t^{4}v(x_{i}^{d})^{2}}{4d^{2}},

(123)

which goes to zero when
d\to\infty
,
for all
\bm{x}^{d}\in H_{d,2}
. The result then follows.

(iii)

Let
\chi:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
be a bounded and continuous function. Define
the sequence
\{\bm{x}^{d}\,:\,d\in\mathbb{N}^{*}\}
, where, for any
d\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
,
\bm{x}^{d}\in H_{d}
satisfies,

\displaystyle\sup_{y^{d}\in H_{d}}\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{Q}_{1}^{d}(y_{i}^{d};\mathrm{d}u)\chi(u)-\left(2\pi\ell^{4}K_{1}^{2}(r)\right)^{-1/2}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\chi(u)e^{-u^{2}/(2\ell^{4}K_{1}^{2}(r))}\mathrm{d}u\right|

(124)

\displaystyle\leq\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{Q}_{1}^{d}(x_{i}^{d};\mathrm{d}u)\chi(u)-\left(2\pi\ell^{4}K_{1}^{2}(r)\right)^{-1/2}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\chi(u)e^{-u^{2}/(2\ell^{4}K_{1}^{2}(r))}\mathrm{d}u\right|+d^{-1}.

(125)

Then, using
(ii)
and Lévy’s continuity theorem (e.g.
[
45
, Theorem 1, page 322]
), we obtain

\lim_{d\to\infty}\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{Q}_{1}^{d}(x_{i}^{d};\mathrm{d}u)\chi(u)-\left(2\pi\ell^{4}K_{1}^{2}(r)\right)^{-1/2}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\chi(u)e^{-u^{2}/(2\ell^{4}K_{1}^{2}(r))}\mathrm{d}u\right|=0.

(126)

Combining this limit with the definition of the sequence
\{\bm{x}^{d}\,:\,d\in\mathbb{N}^{*}\}
, we conclude the proof.

(iv)

This statement follows directly from (iii) and
[
36
, Proposition 2.4]
.

∎

A.3
Auxiliary results for the proof of case
(b)

First, we characterize the limit behaviour of the acceptance ratio (
16
). The following result is an extension of
[
37
, Lemma 1]
.

Proposition 7
.

Under
0
,
1
,
2
and
3
, if
\alpha=1/6
,
\beta=1/6
and
r>0
, then

(i)

the log-acceptance ratio (
42
), when
d\to\infty
, satisfies the following Taylor expansion

\displaystyle\phi_{d}(x,z)

\displaystyle=d^{-1/2}C_{3}(x,z)+d^{-2/3}C_{4}(x,z)

(127)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+d^{-5/6}C_{5}(x,z)+d^{-1}C_{6}(x,z)+C_{7}(x,z,\sigma_{d}),

(128)

where
C_{3}
is given in (
133
),
C_{4},C_{5},C_{6}

are polynomials in
z
and the derivatives of
g
, such that

\mathbb{E}[C_{j}(X_{0,1}^{d},Z_{1,1}^{d})]=0
for

j=3,4,5
and
\mathbb{E}[C_{3}(X^{d}_{0,1},Z^{d}_{1,1})^{2}]=-2\mathbb{E}[C_{6}(X^{d}_{0,1},Z^{d}_{1,1})]
,

(ii)

there exists sets
F_{d}\subseteq\real^{d}
with
d^{2\alpha}\pi_{d}(F_{d}^{c})\to 0
such that

\displaystyle\lim_{d\to\infty}\sup_{\bm{x}^{d}\in F_{d}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lvert\sum_{i=2}^{d}\phi_{d}(x_{i}^{d},Z^{d}_{1,i})-d^{-1/2}\sum_{i=2}^{d}C_{3}(x_{i}^{d},Z^{d}_{1,i})+\frac{\ell^{6}K_{2}(r)^{2}}{2}\right\rvert\right]=0,

(129)

where
K_{2}(r)
is given in
Theorem

1
–
(b)
.

Proof.

Take one component of the log-acceptance ratio

\displaystyle\phi_{d}(x,z)=g(x)-g(y_{d}(x,z))+\log q(y_{d}(x,z),x)-\log q(x,y_{d}(x,z)),

(130)

with
y_{d}(x,z)=x-\sigma_{d}^{2}g^{\prime}(\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}_{d}r/2}(x))+\sigma_{d}z
.
Proceeding as in the proof for case
(a)
, we have that

\phi_{d}(x,z)=R_{1}(x,z,\sigma_{d})+R_{2}(x,z,\sigma_{d})
where
R_{1},R_{2}
are given in (
50
).
Following the approach of
[
37
]
we approximate
\phi_{d}(x,z)
with a Taylor expansion about
\sigma_{d}=0
.

(i)

Using a Taylor expansion of order 7, we find that

\displaystyle\phi_{d}(x,z)

\displaystyle=d^{-1/2}C_{3}(x,z)+d^{-2/3}C_{4}(x,z)+d^{-5/6}C_{5}(x,z)

(131)

\displaystyle+d^{-1}C_{6}(x,z)+C_{7}(x,z,\sigma_{d}),

(132)

where

\displaystyle C_{3}(x,z)

\displaystyle=\frac{\ell^{3}}{6}\left(\frac{1}{2}g^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)z^{3}-\frac{3}{2}g^{\prime\prime}(x)g^{\prime}(x)z\left(1+2r\right)\right),

(133)

C_{4}(x,z),C_{5}(x,z)
and
C_{6}(x,z)
are given in
Section

B.1.2

and integral form of the remainder

\displaystyle C_{7}(x,z,\sigma_{d})=\int_{0}^{\sigma_{d}}\left.\frac{\partial^{7}}{\partial\sigma^{7}}R(x,z,\sigma)\right|_{\sigma=u}\frac{(\sigma_{d}-u)^{6}}{6!}\mathrm{d}u,

(134)

with
u
between 0 and
\sigma_{d}
and the derivatives of
R_{1}
and

R_{2}
are given in Appendix
B.2
.
In addition, integrating by parts and using the moments of the standard
normal
Z^{d}_{1,1}
, we find that
\mathbb{E}[C_{3}(X^{d}_{0,1},Z^{d}_{1,1})]=\mathbb{E}[C_{4}(X^{d}_{0,1},Z^{d}_{1,1})]=\mathbb{E}[C_{5}(X^{d}_{0,1},Z^{d}_{1,1})]=0
and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[C_{6}(X^{d}_{0,1},Z^{d}_{1,1})\right]

\displaystyle=-\frac{\ell^{6}}{16}\left(r+2r^{2}\right)\mathbb{E}\left[\left(g^{\prime\prime}(X^{d}_{0,1})g^{\prime}(X^{d}_{0,1})\right)^{2}\right]

(135)

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{\ell^{6}}{16}\left(\frac{1}{2}+r\right)\mathbb{E}\left[g^{\prime\prime}(X^{d}_{0,1})^{3}\right]-\frac{5\ell^{6}}{96}\mathbb{E}\left[g^{\prime\prime\prime}(X^{d}_{0,1})^{2}\right],

(136)

which shows that
\mathbb{E}[C_{3}(X^{d}_{0,1},Z^{d}_{1,1})^{2}+2C_{6}(X^{d}_{0,1},Z^{d}_{1,1})]=0
.

(ii)

Proposition 8
.

,

,

and

\displaystyle\lim_{d\to\infty}\sup_{\bm{x}^{d}\in S_{d}}\left\lvert\mathrm{L}_{d}V(\bm{x}^{d})-\widetilde{\mathrm{L}}_{d}V(\bm{x}^{d})\right\rvert=0,

(137)

and

\displaystyle\lim_{d\to\infty}\sup_{\bm{x}^{d}\in S_{d}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lvert\left(\exp\left(\sum_{i=1}^{d}\phi_{d}(x^{d}_{i},Z^{d}_{1,i})\right)\wedge 1\right)-\left(\exp\left(\sum_{i=2}^{d}\phi_{d}(x^{d}_{i},Z^{d}_{1,i})\right)\wedge 1\right)\right\rvert\right]=0.

(138)

Proof.

Proceeding as in Proposition
4
,
for any
V\in\mathrm{C}_{\mathrm{c}}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R},\mathbb{R})
,
there exists a constant
C
such that

\displaystyle\left|V\left(y_{d}(x^{d}_{1},Z^{d}_{1,1})\right)-V(x_{1}^{d})\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\left|y_{d}(x^{d}_{1},Z^{d}_{1,1})-x_{1}^{d}\right|

(139)

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\sigma_{d}|Z_{1,1}^{d}|+\frac{\sigma_{d}^{2}}{2}\left|g^{\prime}\left(\prox_{g}^{\sigma_{d}^{2v}r/2}(x_{1}^{d})\right)\right|\right).

(140)

Under
3
,
g^{\prime}
is Lipschitz continuous and we have, for some
C\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\left|g^{\prime}\left(\prox_{g}^{\sigma_{d}^{2v}r/2}(x_{1}^{d})\right)\right|\leq C\left(1+\left|\prox_{g}^{\sigma_{d}^{2v}r/2}(x_{1}^{d})\right|\right)\leq C(1+|x_{1}^{d}|),

(141)

where we used the fact that
\prox_{g}^{\lambda}
is
1
-Lipschitz continuous for all
\lambda>0
.
The result then follows similarly to
4
and

[
37
, Theorem 3]
.

A.5
Proof of Theorem
1

Proof of Theorem
1
.

(a)

The asymptotic acceptance rate follows by combining Propositions
3
–
5
with part (iv) of Proposition
6
as in the proof of
[
37
, Theorem 1]
.
To prove the weak convergence of the process it suffices to show that there exists events
F_{d}^{\star}\in\real^{d}
such that for all
t>0

\displaystyle\lim_{d\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left(L_{t}^{d}\in F_{d}^{\star}\textrm{ for all
}0\leq s\leq t\right)=1,

(163)

and

\displaystyle\lim_{d\to\infty}\sup_{\bm{x}^{d}\in F_{d}^{\star}}\left|\mathrm{L}_{d}V(\bm{x}^{d})-\mathrm{L}V(\bm{x}^{d})\right\rvert.

(164)

for all
V\in\mathrm{C}_{\mathrm{c}}^{\infty}(\real,\real)

[
19
, Chapter 4, Corollary 8.7]
.
We take
F_{d}^{\star}=F_{d}\cap S_{d}\cap T_{d}\cap H_{d}
. Then,
d^{2\alpha}\pi_{d}\left((F_{d}^{\star})^{c}\right)\to 0
and

\displaystyle\lim_{d\to\infty}\mathbb{P}\left(L_{t}^{d}\in F_{d}^{\star}\textrm{ for all }0\leq s\leq t\right)=1,

(165)

for all fixed
t
. Combining Propositions
3
–
6
we obtain convergence of the generators.

To obtain the value of
a(\ell,r)
maximizing the speed, we observe that
K_{1}^{2}(r)
is a function of the ratio
r=c^{2}/\ell^{2v}=c^{2}/\ell
only, we can take
c\propto\ell^{1/2}
so that
K_{1}^{2}(r)
is constant for given
c
.
Using the same substitution as in
[
37
, Theorem 2]
we find that
h(\ell,r)
is maximized at the unique value of
\ell
such that
a(\ell,r)=0.452
.

(b)

The proof is analogous to that of case
(a)
replacing Propositions
3
,
4
,
5
and
6
with Propositions
7
,
8
,
9
and
10
.
To obtain the value of
a(\ell,r)
maximizing the speed, we observe that
K_{2}^{2}(r)
is a function of the ratio
r=c^{2}/\ell^{2v}=c^{2}/\ell^{2}
only, we can take
c\propto\ell
so that
K_{2}^{2}(r)
is constant for given
c
.
Using the same substitution as in
[
37
, Theorem 2]
we find that
h(\ell,r)
is maximized at the unique value of
\ell
such that
a(\ell,r)=0.574
.

(a)
replacing
Propositions
3
,
4
,

5
and
6
with
Propositions
11
,
12
,

and
14
. To obtain the value of

a(\ell,r)
maximizing the speed, we observe that
K_{2}^{2}(0)
is
constant w.r.t.
r
, we can use the same substitution as in

[
37
, Theorem 2]
we find that

is

such that

If
\alpha=1/4
,
\beta=1/8
and
r>0
, then the log-acceptance ratio (
42
) satisfies

\displaystyle\phi_{d}(x,z)

\displaystyle=d^{-1/2}C_{2}(x,z)+d^{-3/4}C_{3}(x,z)+d^{-1}C_{4}(x,z)+C_{5}(x,z,\sigma_{d}),

(166)

where

\displaystyle C_{2}(x,z)

\displaystyle=\frac{\ell^{2}}{2}\left(-rzg^{\prime\prime}(x)g^{\prime}(x)\right),

(167)

and

\displaystyle C_{3}(x,z)

\displaystyle=\frac{\ell^{3}}{6}\left(\frac{z^{3}}{2}g^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)-\frac{3}{2}z^{2}rg^{\prime}(x)g^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)-\frac{3}{2}rz^{2}\left[g^{\prime\prime}(x)\right]^{2}+\frac{3}{4}zr^{2}g^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)\left[g^{\prime}(x)\right]^{2}\right.

(168)

\displaystyle\left.-\frac{3}{2}zg^{\prime}(x)g^{\prime\prime}(x)+\frac{3}{2}r\left[g^{\prime}(x)\right]^{2}g^{\prime\prime}(x)+3r^{2}z[g^{\prime}(x)]^{2}g^{\prime\prime}(x)\right),

(169)

\displaystyle C_{4}(x,z)

\displaystyle=\frac{\ell^{4}}{24}\left\{g^{(4)}(x)\left(z^{4}-\frac{zr^{3}}{2}\left[g^{\prime}(x)\right]^{3}-3z^{3}rg^{\prime}(x)+\frac{3}{2}z^{2}r^{2}\left[g^{\prime}(x)\right]^{2}\right)\right.

(170)

\displaystyle+g^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)\left(-6z^{2}g^{\prime}(x)-9rz^{3}g^{\prime\prime}(x)+9z^{2}r^{2}g^{\prime}(x)g^{\prime\prime}(x)+9rz\left[g^{\prime}(x)\right]^{2}\right.

(171)

\displaystyle\qquad\left.-\frac{9}{2}zr^{3}\left[g^{\prime}(x)\right]^{2}g^{\prime\prime}(x)-\frac{3}{2}r^{2}\left[g^{\prime}(x)\right]^{3}\right)

(172)

\displaystyle+12rzg^{\prime}(x)\left[g^{\prime\prime}(x)\right]^{2}+3g^{\prime\prime}(x)\left[g^{\prime}(x)\right]^{2}-3z^{2}\left[g^{\prime\prime}(x)\right]^{2}+3z^{2}r^{2}\left[g^{\prime\prime}(x)\right]^{3}

(173)

\displaystyle\qquad\left.-6r^{2}\left[g^{\prime}(x)g^{\prime\prime}(x)\right]^{2}-3zr^{3}g^{\prime}(x)\left[g^{\prime\prime}(x)\right]^{3}\right\},

(174)

and we use the integral form for the remainder

\displaystyle C_{5}(x,z,\sigma_{d})=\int_{0}^{\sigma_{d}}\left.\frac{\partial^{5}}{\partial\sigma^{5}}R(x,z,\sigma)\right|_{\sigma=u}\frac{(\sigma_{d}-u)^{4}}{4!}\mathrm{d}u,

(175)

with
u
between 0 and
\sigma_{d}
and the derivatives of
R_{1}
and
R_{2}
given in Appendix
B.2
.

B.1.2
Case
(b)

If
\alpha=1/6
,
\beta=1/6
and
r>0
, the the log-acceptance ratio (
42
) satisfies

\displaystyle\phi_{d}(x,z)

\displaystyle=d^{-1/2}C_{3}(x,z)+d^{-2/3}C_{4}(x,z)

(176)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+d^{-5/6}C_{5}(x,z)+d^{-1}C_{6}(x,z)+C_{7}(x,z,\sigma_{d}),

(177)

where

\displaystyle C_{3}(x,z)

\displaystyle=\frac{\ell^{3}}{6}\left(\frac{1}{2}g^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)z^{3}-\frac{3}{2}g^{\prime\prime}(x)g^{\prime}(x)z\left(1+2r\right)\right),

(178)

and

\displaystyle C_{4}(x,z)

\displaystyle=\frac{\ell^{4}}{24}\left(z^{4}g^{(4)}(x)-6z^{2}g^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)g^{\prime}(x)(1+r)\right.

(179)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\left.-3z^{2}\left[g^{\prime\prime}(x)\right]^{2}(1+2r)+3g^{\prime\prime}(x)\left[g^{\prime}(x)\right]^{2}(1+2r)\right),

(180)

\displaystyle C_{5}(x,z)

\displaystyle=\frac{\ell^{5}}{120}\left(\frac{3}{2}z^{5}g^{(5)}(x)-15z^{3}g^{(4)}(x)g^{\prime}(x)(1+r)+15z\left[g^{\prime}(x)\right]^{2}g^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)\left(\frac{3}{2}+3r+r^{2}\right)\right.

(181)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\left.+15z(1+4r+2r^{2})g^{\prime}(x)\left[g^{\prime\prime}(x)\right]^{2}-15z^{3}g^{\prime\prime}(x)g^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)(1+3r)\right),

(182)

\displaystyle C_{6}(x,z)

\displaystyle=\frac{\ell^{6}}{720}\left(2z^{6}g^{(6)}(x)-30\left(1+r\right)z^{4}g^{\prime}(x)g^{(5)}(x)+45\left(2+4r+r^{2}\right)z^{2}\left[g^{\prime}(x)\right]^{2}g^{(4)}(x)\right.

(183)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+90\left(r+r^{2}\right)z^{2}\left[g^{\prime\prime}(x)\right]^{3}-15\left(2+6r+3r^{2}\right)g^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)\left[g^{\prime}(x)\right]^{3}

(184)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-30\left(1+4r\right)z^{4}g^{\prime\prime}(x)g^{(4)}(x)+45\left(3+16r+6r^{2}\right)z^{2}g^{\prime}(x)g^{\prime\prime}(x)g^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)

(185)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\left.-\frac{45}{2}\left(1+4r\right)z^{4}\left[g^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)\right]^{2}-\frac{45}{2}\left(1+8r+8r^{2}\right)\left[g^{\prime}(x)g^{\prime\prime}(x)\right]^{2}\right),

(186)

and integral form of the remainder

\displaystyle C_{7}(x,z,\sigma_{d})=\int_{0}^{\sigma_{d}}\left.\frac{\partial^{7}}{\partial\sigma^{7}}R(x,z,\sigma)\right|_{\sigma=u}\frac{(\sigma_{d}-u)^{6}}{6!}\mathrm{d}u,

(187)

with
u
between 0 and
\sigma_{d}
and the derivatives of
R_{1}
and
R_{2}
are given in Appendix
B.2
.

B.1.3
Case
(c)

,

for

and
r>0
, then the log-acceptance ratio (
42
) satisfies

\displaystyle\phi_{d}(x,z)

\displaystyle=d^{-1/2}C_{3}(x,z)+d^{-2/3}C_{4}(x,z)

(188)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+d^{-5/6}C_{5}(x,z)+d^{-1}C_{6}(x,z)+C_{7}(x,z,\sigma_{d}),

(189)

where

\displaystyle C_{3}(x,z)

\displaystyle=\frac{\ell^{3}}{6}\left(\frac{1}{2}g^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)z^{3}-\frac{3}{2}g^{\prime\prime}(x)g^{\prime}(x)z\right)

(190)

and

\displaystyle C_{4}(x,z)

\displaystyle=\frac{\ell^{4}}{24}\begin{cases}z^{4}g^{(4)}(x)-6z^{2}g^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)g^{\prime}(x)-3z^{2}\left[g^{\prime\prime}(x)\right]^{2}+3g^{\prime\prime}(x)\left[g^{\prime}(x)\right]^{2}\\
\qquad\qquad-12zrg^{\prime}(x)g^{\prime\prime}(x)\qquad\qquad\textrm{if }v=3/2\\
z^{4}g^{(4)}(x)-6z^{2}g^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)g^{\prime}(x)-3z^{2}\left[g^{\prime\prime}(x)\right]^{2}+3g^{\prime\prime}(x)\left[g^{\prime}(x)\right]^{2}\\
\qquad\qquad\textrm{otherwise }\\
\end{cases},

(191)

\displaystyle C_{5}(x,z)

\displaystyle=\frac{\ell^{5}}{120}\begin{cases}\frac{3}{2}z^{5}g^{(5)}(x)-15z^{3}g^{(4)}(x)g^{\prime}(x)+\frac{45}{2}z\left[g^{\prime}(x)\right]^{2}g^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)+15zg^{\prime}(x)\left[g^{\prime\prime}(x)\right]^{2}\\
\qquad-15z^{3}g^{\prime\prime}(x)g^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)-30z^{2}r\left[g^{\prime\prime}(x)\right]^{2}+30r\left[g^{\prime}(x)\right]^{2}g^{\prime\prime}(x)\\
\qquad-30rz^{2}g^{\prime}(x)g^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)\\
\qquad\qquad\qquad\textrm{if }v=3/2\\
\frac{3}{2}z^{5}g^{(5)}(x)-15z^{3}g^{(4)}(x)g^{\prime}(x)+\frac{45}{2}z\left[g^{\prime}(x)\right]^{2}g^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)+15zg^{\prime}(x)\left[g^{\prime\prime}(x)\right]^{2}\\
\qquad-15z^{3}g^{\prime\prime}(x)g^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)-60zrg^{\prime}(x)g^{\prime\prime}(x)\\
\qquad\qquad\qquad\textrm{if }v=2\\
\frac{3}{2}z^{5}g^{(5)}(x)-15z^{3}g^{(4)}(x)g^{\prime}(x)+\frac{45}{2}z\left[g^{\prime}(x)\right]^{2}g^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)\\
\qquad+15zg^{\prime}(x)\left[g^{\prime\prime}(x)\right]^{2}-15z^{3}g^{\prime\prime}(x)g^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)\qquad\qquad\qquad\textrm{otherwise }\end{cases},

(192)

\displaystyle C_{6}(x,z)

\displaystyle=\frac{\ell^{6}}{720}\begin{cases}2z^{6}g^{(6)}(x)-30z^{4}g^{\prime}(x)g^{(5)}(x)+90z^{2}\left[g^{\prime}(x)\right]^{2}g^{(4)}(x)-30g^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)\left[g^{\prime}(x)\right]^{3}\\
\qquad-\frac{45}{2}\left[g^{\prime}(x)g^{\prime\prime}(x)\right]^{2}-30z^{4}g^{\prime\prime}(x)g^{(4)}(x)+135z^{2}g^{\prime}(x)g^{\prime\prime}(x)g^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)\\
\qquad-\frac{45}{2}z^{4}\left[g^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)\right]^{2}-90z^{3}rg^{\prime}(x)g^{(4)}(x)+540rzg^{\prime}(x)\left[g^{\prime\prime}(x)\right]^{2}\\
\qquad+180rzg^{\prime}(x)g^{\prime\prime}(x)+270rzg^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)\left[g^{\prime}(x)\right]^{2}-45zrg^{\prime\prime}(x)g^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)\\
\qquad+90rz^{3}g^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)\\
\qquad\qquad\qquad\textrm{if }v=3/2\\
2z^{6}g^{(6)}(x)-30z^{4}g^{\prime}(x)g^{(5)}(x)+90z^{2}\left[g^{\prime}(x)\right]^{2}g^{(4)}(x)-30g^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)\left[g^{\prime}(x)\right]^{3}\\
\qquad-\frac{45}{2}\left[g^{\prime}(x)g^{\prime\prime}(x)\right]^{2}-30z^{4}g^{\prime\prime}(x)g^{(4)}(x)+135z^{2}g^{\prime}(x)g^{\prime\prime}(x)g^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)\\
\qquad-\frac{45}{2}z^{4}\left[g^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)\right]^{2}-180z^{2}rg^{\prime}(x)g^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)-180z^{2}r\left[g^{\prime\prime}(x)\right]^{2}\\
\qquad+180rg^{\prime\prime}(x)\left[g^{\prime}(x)\right]^{2}\\
\qquad\qquad\qquad\textrm{if }v=2\\
2z^{6}g^{(6)}(x)-30z^{4}g^{\prime}(x)g^{(5)}(x)+90z^{2}\left[g^{\prime}(x)\right]^{2}g^{(4)}(x)-30g^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)\left[g^{\prime}(x)\right]^{3}\\
\qquad-\frac{45}{2}\left[g^{\prime}(x)g^{\prime\prime}(x)\right]^{2}-30z^{4}g^{\prime\prime}(x)g^{(4)}(x)+135z^{2}g^{\prime}(x)g^{\prime\prime}(x)g^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)\\
\qquad-\frac{45}{2}z^{4}\left[g^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)\right]^{2}-360zrg^{\prime}(x)g^{\prime\prime}(x)\\
\qquad\qquad\qquad\textrm{if }v=5/2\\
2z^{6}g^{(6)}(x)-30z^{4}g^{\prime}(x)g^{(5)}(x)+90z^{2}\left[g^{\prime}(x)\right]^{2}g^{(4)}(x)-30g^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)\left[g^{\prime}(x)\right]^{3}\\
\qquad-\frac{45}{2}\left[g^{\prime}(x)g^{\prime\prime}(x)\right]^{2}-30z^{4}g^{\prime\prime}(x)g^{(4)}(x)+135z^{2}g^{\prime}(x)g^{\prime\prime}(x)g^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)\\
\qquad-\frac{45}{2}z^{4}\left[g^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)\right]^{2}\\
\qquad\qquad\qquad\textrm{otherwise}\\
\end{cases},

(193)

and integral form of the remainder

\displaystyle C_{7}(x,z,\sigma_{d})=\int_{0}^{\sigma_{d}}\left.\frac{\partial^{7}}{\partial\sigma^{7}}R(x,z,\sigma)\right|_{\sigma=u}\frac{(\sigma_{d}-u)^{6}}{6!}\mathrm{d}u,

(194)

with
u
between 0 and
\sigma_{d}
and the derivatives of
R_{1}
and
R_{2}
are given in Appendix
B.2
.

B.2
Taylor expansions of the log-acceptance ratio

B.2.1
R_{1}

Recall that
R_{1}(x,z,\sigma)=-g\left[x-\frac{\sigma^{2}}{2}g^{\prime}\left(\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)\right)+\sigma z\right]+g(x)
. We compute the derivatives of
R_{1}
w.r.t.
\sigma
evaluated at 0:

\displaystyle R_{1}(x,z,0)

\displaystyle=0,

(195)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial R_{1}}{\partial\sigma}(x,z,\sigma)_{\mid\sigma=0}

\displaystyle=-g^{\prime}(x)z,

(196)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{2}R_{1}}{\partial\sigma^{2}}(x,z,\sigma)_{\mid\sigma=0}

\displaystyle=-z^{2}g^{\prime\prime}(x)+\left[g^{\prime}(x)\right]^{2},

(197)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{3}R_{1}}{\partial\sigma^{3}}(x,z,\sigma)_{\mid\sigma=0}

\displaystyle=-z^{3}g^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)+3g^{\prime}(x)g^{\prime\prime}(x)\left[z+\frac{\partial}{\partial\sigma}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}\right],

(198)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{4}R_{1}}{\partial\sigma^{4}}(x,z,\sigma)_{\mid\sigma=0}

\displaystyle=-z^{4}g^{(4)}(x)+6z^{2}g^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)g^{\prime}(x)-3g^{\prime\prime}(x)\left[g^{\prime}(x)\right]^{2}

(199)

\displaystyle+12z\left[g^{\prime\prime}(x)\right]^{2}\frac{\partial}{\partial\sigma}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}

(200)

\displaystyle+6g^{\prime}(x)

(201)

\displaystyle\times\left[g^{\prime\prime}(x)\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\sigma^{2}}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}+\left(\frac{\partial}{\partial\sigma}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}\right)^{2}g^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)\right].

(202)

In addition, for
v>1/2
we will also use

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{5}R_{1}}{\partial\sigma^{5}}(x,z,\sigma)_{\mid\sigma=0}

\displaystyle=-z^{5}g^{(5)}(x)+10z^{3}g^{(4)}(x)g^{\prime}(x)-15zg^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)\left[g^{\prime}(x)\right]^{2}

(203)

\displaystyle+30z\left[g^{\prime\prime}(x)\right]^{2}\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial^{2}\sigma}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}

(204)

\displaystyle+10g^{\prime}(x)g^{\prime\prime}(x)\frac{\partial^{3}}{\partial^{3}\sigma}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0},

(205)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{6}R_{1}}{\partial\sigma^{6}}(x,z,\sigma)_{\mid\sigma=0}

\displaystyle=-z^{6}g^{(6)}(x)+15z^{4}g^{(5)}(x)g^{\prime}(x)-45z^{2}g^{(4)}(x)\left[g^{\prime}(x)\right]^{2}+15g^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)\left[g^{\prime}(x)\right]^{3}

(206)

\displaystyle-90g^{\prime}(x)\left[g^{\prime\prime}(x)\right]^{2}\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\sigma^{2}}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}

(207)

\displaystyle-60zg^{\prime\prime}(x)\frac{\partial^{3}}{\partial\sigma^{3}}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}

(208)

\displaystyle+90g^{\prime\prime}(x)g^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\sigma^{2}}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}

(209)

\displaystyle+15g^{\prime}(x)

(210)

\displaystyle\times\left(g^{\prime\prime}(x)\frac{\partial^{4}}{\partial\sigma^{4}}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}+3\left[\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\sigma^{2}}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}\right]^{2}g^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)\right).

(211)

B.2.2
R_{2}

Recall that

\displaystyle R_{2}(x,z,\sigma)=\frac{1}{2}z^{2}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\left(z-\frac{\sigma}{2}g^{\prime}\left(\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}\left[x+\sigma z-\frac{\sigma^{2}}{2}g^{\prime}\left[\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)\right]\right]\right)-\frac{\sigma}{2}g^{\prime}\left[\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)\right]\right)^{2}.

We compute the derivatives of
R_{2}
w.r.t.
\sigma
evaluated at 0:

\displaystyle R_{2}(x,z,0)

\displaystyle=0,

(212)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial R_{2}}{\partial\sigma}(x,z,\sigma)_{|\sigma=0}

\displaystyle=zg^{\prime}(x),

(213)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{2}R_{2}}{\partial\sigma^{2}}(x,z,\sigma)_{|\sigma=0}

\displaystyle=-\left[g^{\prime}(x)\right]^{2}+zg^{\prime\prime}(x)\left[z+2\frac{\partial}{\partial\sigma}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}\right],

(214)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{3}R_{2}}{\partial\sigma^{3}}(x,z,\sigma)_{|\sigma=0}

\displaystyle=-3g^{\prime}(x)g^{\prime\prime}(x)\left[z+2\frac{\partial}{\partial\sigma}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}\right]

(215)

\displaystyle+\frac{3}{2}z\left(\left[z+\frac{\partial}{\partial\sigma}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}\right]^{2}g^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)\right.

(216)

\displaystyle\left.+\left[-g^{\prime}(x)+2z\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\sigma\partial x}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}+\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\sigma^{2}}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}\right]g^{\prime\prime}(x)\right.

(217)

\displaystyle\left.+\left[\frac{\partial}{\partial\sigma}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}\right]^{2}g^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)+\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\sigma^{2}}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}g^{\prime\prime}(x)\right),

(218)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{4}R_{2}}{\partial\sigma^{4}}(x,z,\sigma)_{|\sigma=0}

\displaystyle=-3\left[g^{\prime\prime}(x)\right]^{2}\left[z+2\frac{\partial}{\partial\sigma}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}\right]^{2}

(219)

\displaystyle-6g^{\prime}(x)\left(\left[z+\frac{\partial}{\partial\sigma}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}\right]^{2}g^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)\right.

(220)

\displaystyle\left.+\left[-g^{\prime}(x)+2z\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\sigma\partial x}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}+\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\sigma^{2}}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}\right]g^{\prime\prime}(x)\right.

(221)

\displaystyle\left.+\left[\frac{\partial}{\partial\sigma}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}\right]^{2}g^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)+\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\sigma^{2}}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}g^{\prime\prime}(x)\right)

(222)

\displaystyle+2zg^{(4)}(x)\left[z+\frac{\partial}{\partial\sigma}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}\right]^{3}

(223)

\displaystyle+6zg^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)\left[z+\frac{\partial}{\partial\sigma}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}\right]

(224)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\left[-g^{\prime}(x)+2z\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\sigma\partial x}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}+\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\sigma^{2}}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}\right]

(225)

\displaystyle+2zg^{\prime\prime}(x)\left[-3g^{\prime\prime}(x)\frac{\partial}{\partial\sigma}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}-3g^{\prime}(x)\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\sigma\partial x}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}\right.

(226)

\displaystyle\left.\qquad+3z^{2}\frac{\partial^{3}}{\partial\sigma\partial x^{2}}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}+3z\frac{\partial^{3}}{\partial\sigma^{2}\partial x}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}\right.

(227)

\displaystyle\qquad\left.+\frac{\partial^{3}}{\partial\sigma^{3}}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}\right]

(228)

\displaystyle+2zg^{(4)}(x)\left[\frac{\partial}{\partial\sigma}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}\right]^{3}+2zg^{\prime\prime}(x)\frac{\partial^{3}}{\partial\sigma^{3}}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}

(229)

\displaystyle+6z\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\sigma^{2}}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}\frac{\partial}{\partial\sigma}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}g^{\prime\prime\prime}(x).

(230)

We then proceed to get the derivatives needed for
v>1/2
:

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{5}R_{2}}{\partial\sigma^{5}}(x,z,\sigma)_{|\sigma=0}

\displaystyle=-15zg^{\prime\prime}(x)\left(z^{2}g^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)+\left[-g^{\prime}(x)+2\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\sigma^{2}}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}\right]g^{\prime\prime}(x)\right)

(231)

\displaystyle-5g^{\prime}(x)\left(2z^{3}g^{(4)}(x)+6zg^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)\left[-g^{\prime}(x)+\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\sigma^{2}}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}\right]\right.

(232)

\displaystyle\left.\qquad+2g^{\prime\prime}(x)\left[3z\frac{\partial^{3}}{\partial\sigma^{2}\partial x}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}+\frac{\partial^{3}}{\partial\sigma^{3}}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}\right]\right.

(233)

\displaystyle\left.\qquad+2g^{\prime\prime}(x)\frac{\partial^{3}}{\partial\sigma^{3}}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}\right)

(234)

\displaystyle+\frac{5}{2}g^{(5)}(x)z^{5}+\frac{5}{2}zg^{\prime\prime}(x)\frac{\partial^{4}}{\partial\sigma^{4}}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}

(235)

\displaystyle+15g^{(4)}(x)z^{3}\left[-g^{\prime}(x)+\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\sigma^{2}}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}\right]

(236)

\displaystyle+\frac{15}{2}zg^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)\left[-g^{\prime}(x)+\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\sigma^{2}}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}\right]^{2}

(237)

\displaystyle+\frac{15}{2}zg^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)\left[\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\sigma^{2}}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}\right]^{2}

(238)

\displaystyle+10z^{2}g^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)\left(3z\frac{\partial^{3}}{\partial\sigma^{2}\partial x}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}+\frac{\partial^{3}}{\partial\sigma^{3}}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}\right)

(239)

\displaystyle+\frac{5}{2}zg^{\prime\prime}(x)\left(\frac{\partial^{4}}{\partial\sigma^{4}}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}-6g^{\prime\prime}(x)\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\sigma^{2}}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}\right.

(240)

\displaystyle\qquad\left.-6g^{\prime}(x)\frac{\partial^{3}}{\partial\sigma^{2}\partial x}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}+6z^{2}\frac{\partial^{4}}{\partial\sigma^{2}\partial x^{2}}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}\right.

(241)

\displaystyle\left.\qquad+4z\frac{\partial^{4}}{\partial\sigma^{3}\partial x}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}\right)

(242)

and

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{6}R_{2}}{\partial\sigma^{6}}(x,z,\sigma)_{|\sigma=0}

\displaystyle=-\frac{45}{2}\left(z^{2}g^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)+\left[-g^{\prime}(x)+2\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\sigma^{2}}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}\right]g^{\prime\prime}(x)\right)^{2}

(243)

\displaystyle-15zg^{\prime\prime}(x)\left(2z^{3}g^{(4)}(x)+6zg^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)\left[-g^{\prime}(x)+\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\sigma^{2}}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}\right]\right.

(244)

\displaystyle\qquad+2g^{\prime\prime}(x)\left[3z\frac{\partial^{3}}{\partial\sigma^{2}\partial x}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}+\frac{\partial^{3}}{\partial\sigma^{3}}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}\right]

(245)

\displaystyle\qquad\left.+2g^{\prime\prime}(x)\frac{\partial^{3}}{\partial\sigma^{3}}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}\right)

(246)

\displaystyle+6g^{\prime}(x)A^{(5)}-zA^{(6)},

(247)

with

\displaystyle A^{(5)}

\displaystyle=-\frac{5}{2}g^{(5)}(x)z^{4}-\frac{5}{2}g^{\prime\prime}(x)\frac{\partial^{4}}{\partial\sigma^{4}}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}

(248)

\displaystyle-15g^{(4)}(x)z^{2}\left[-g^{\prime}(x)+\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\sigma^{2}}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}\right]

(249)

\displaystyle-\frac{15}{2}g^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)\left[-g^{\prime}(x)+\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\sigma^{2}}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}\right]^{2}-\frac{15}{2}g^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)\left[\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\sigma^{2}}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}\right]^{2}

(250)

\displaystyle-10zg^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)\left(3z\frac{\partial^{3}}{\partial\sigma^{2}\partial x}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}+\frac{\partial^{3}}{\partial\sigma^{3}}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}\right)

(251)

\displaystyle-\frac{5}{2}g^{\prime\prime}(x)\left(\frac{\partial^{4}}{\partial\sigma^{4}}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}-6g^{\prime\prime}(x)\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\sigma^{2}}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}\right.

(252)

\displaystyle\qquad\left.-6g^{\prime}(x)\frac{\partial^{3}}{\partial\sigma^{2}\partial x}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}\right.

(253)

\displaystyle\qquad\left.+6z^{2}\frac{\partial^{4}}{\partial\sigma^{2}\partial x^{2}}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}+4z\frac{\partial^{4}}{\partial\sigma^{3}\partial x}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}\right),

(254)

\displaystyle A^{(6)}

\displaystyle=-3\left(10g^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\sigma^{2}}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}\frac{\partial^{3}}{\partial\sigma^{3}}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}\right.

(255)

\displaystyle\left.+g^{\prime\prime}(x)\frac{\partial^{5}}{\partial\sigma^{5}}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}+g^{(6)}(x)z^{5}\right.

(256)

\displaystyle+10g^{(5)}(x)z^{3}\left[-g^{\prime}(x)+\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\sigma^{2}}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}\right]

(257)

\displaystyle+15zg^{(4)}(x)\left[-g^{\prime}(x)+\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\sigma^{2}}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}\right]^{2}

(258)

\displaystyle+10g^{(4)}(x)z^{2}\left[\frac{\partial^{3}}{\partial\sigma^{3}}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}+3z\frac{\partial^{3}}{\partial\sigma^{2}\partial x}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}\right]

(259)

\displaystyle+10g^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)\left[-g^{\prime}(x)+\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\sigma^{2}}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}\right]

(260)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\left[\frac{\partial^{3}}{\partial\sigma^{3}}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}+3z\frac{\partial^{3}}{\partial\sigma^{2}\partial x}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}\right]

(261)

\displaystyle+5g^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)z\left[-6g^{\prime\prime}(x)\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\sigma^{2}}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}-6g^{\prime}(x)\frac{\partial^{3}}{\partial\sigma^{2}\partial x}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}\right.

(262)

\displaystyle\qquad\left.+6z^{2}\frac{\partial^{4}}{\partial\sigma^{2}\partial x^{2}}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}+3z\frac{\partial^{4}}{\partial\sigma^{3}\partial x}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}\right.

(263)

\displaystyle\qquad\left.+\frac{\partial^{4}}{\partial\sigma^{4}}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}\right]

(264)

\displaystyle+g^{\prime\prime}(x)\left[-10g^{\prime\prime}(x)\frac{\partial^{3}}{\partial\sigma^{3}}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}-10g^{\prime}(x)\frac{\partial^{4}}{\partial\sigma^{3}\partial x}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}\right.

(265)

\displaystyle\qquad+5z\frac{\partial^{5}}{\partial\sigma^{4}\partial x}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}+10z^{2}\frac{\partial^{5}}{\partial\sigma^{3}\partial x^{2}}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}

(266)

\displaystyle\qquad+10z^{3}\frac{\partial^{5}}{\partial\sigma^{2}\partial x^{3}}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}

(267)

\displaystyle\qquad\left.\left.-30g^{\prime}(x)z\frac{\partial^{4}}{\partial\sigma^{2}\partial x^{2}}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}+\frac{\partial^{5}}{\partial\sigma^{5}}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{\mid\sigma=0}\right]\right).

(268)

B.3
Derivatives of the proximity map for regular targets

Recall that, in the regular case,
\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)=-\frac{\sigma^{2v}r}{2}g^{\prime}(\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x))+x

then

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial\sigma}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{|\sigma=0}=\begin{cases}-\frac{r}{2}g^{\prime}(x)\qquad\textrm{if }v=1/2\\
0\qquad\textrm{if }v>1/2\\
\infty\qquad\textrm{otherwise}\end{cases}

(269)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\sigma^{2}}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{|\sigma=0}=\begin{cases}\frac{r^{2}}{2}g^{\prime}(x)g^{\prime\prime}(x)\qquad\textrm{if }v=1/2\\
-rg^{\prime}(x)\qquad\textrm{if }v=1\\
0\qquad\textrm{if }m>1\\
\infty\qquad\textrm{otherwise}\end{cases}

(270)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{3}}{\partial\sigma^{3}}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{|\sigma=0}=\begin{cases}-\frac{3r^{3}}{8}g^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)\left[g^{\prime}(x)\right]^{2}-\frac{3r^{3}}{4}g^{\prime}(x)\left[g^{\prime\prime}(x)\right]^{2}\qquad\textrm{if }v=1/2\\
-3rg^{\prime}(x)\qquad\textrm{if }v=3/2\\
0\qquad\textrm{if }v=1,m>3/2\\
\infty\qquad\textrm{otherwise}\end{cases}

(271)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{4}}{\partial\sigma^{4}}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{|\sigma=0}=\begin{cases}<\infty\qquad\textrm{if }v=1/2\\
6r^{2}g^{\prime}(x)g^{\prime\prime}(x)\qquad\textrm{if }v=1\\
-12rg^{\prime}(x)\qquad\textrm{if }v=2\\
0\qquad\textrm{if }v=3/2,v>2\\
\infty\qquad\textrm{otherwise}\end{cases}

(272)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{5}}{\partial\sigma^{5}}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{|\sigma=0}=\begin{cases}<\infty\qquad\textrm{if }v=1/2\\
-60rg^{\prime}(x)\qquad\textrm{if }v=5/2\\
0\qquad\textrm{if }v=1,v=3/2,v=2,v>5/2\\
\infty\qquad\textrm{otherwise}\end{cases}

(273)

and

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial x}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{|\sigma=0}=1,\qquad\qquad\qquad\frac{\partial^{(k)}}{\partial x^{(k)}}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{|\sigma=0}=0,

(275)

for all integers
k>1
.
For the mixed derivatives we have

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\sigma\partial x}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{|\sigma=0}=\begin{cases}-\frac{r}{2}g^{\prime\prime}(x)\qquad\textrm{if }v=1/2\\
0\qquad\textrm{if }v>1/2\\
\infty\qquad\textrm{otherwise}\end{cases}

(276)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{3}}{\partial\sigma^{2}\partial x}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{|\sigma=0}=\begin{cases}\frac{r^{2}}{2}\left[g^{\prime\prime}(x)\right]^{2}+\frac{r^{2}}{2}g^{\prime}(x)g^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)\qquad\textrm{if }v=1/2\\
-rg^{\prime\prime}(x)\qquad\textrm{if }v=1\\
0\qquad\textrm{if }v>1/2\\
\infty\qquad\textrm{otherwise}\end{cases}

(277)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{4}}{\partial\sigma^{3}\partial x}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{|\sigma=0}=\begin{cases}<\infty\qquad\textrm{if }v=1/2\\
-3rg^{\prime\prime}(x)\qquad\textrm{if }v=3/2\\
0\qquad\textrm{if }v=1,v>3/2\\
\infty\qquad\textrm{otherwise}\end{cases}

(278)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{5}}{\partial\sigma^{4}\partial x}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{|\sigma=0}=\begin{cases}<\infty\qquad\textrm{if }v=1/2\\
6r^{2}\left(g^{\prime}(x)g^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)+\left[g^{\prime\prime}(x)\right]^{2}\right)\qquad\textrm{if }v=1\\
-12rg^{\prime\prime}(x)\qquad\textrm{if }v=2\\
0\qquad\textrm{if }v=3/2,v>2\\
\infty\qquad\textrm{otherwise}\end{cases}

(279)

and

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{3}}{\partial\sigma\partial x^{2}}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{|\sigma=0}=\begin{cases}-\frac{r}{2}g^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)\qquad\textrm{if }v=1/2\\
0\qquad\textrm{if }v>1/2\\
\infty\qquad\textrm{otherwise}\end{cases}

(281)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{4}}{\partial\sigma\partial x^{3}}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{|\sigma=0}=\begin{cases}<\infty\qquad\textrm{if }v=1/2\\
0\qquad\textrm{if }v>1/2\\
\infty\qquad\textrm{otherwise}\end{cases}

(282)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{4}}{\partial\sigma^{2}\partial x^{2}}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{|\sigma=0}=\begin{cases}<\infty\qquad\textrm{if }v=1/2\\
-rg^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)\qquad\textrm{if }v=1\\
0\qquad\textrm{if }v>1/2\\
\infty\qquad\textrm{otherwise}\end{cases}

(283)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{5}}{\partial\sigma^{3}\partial x^{2}}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{|\sigma=0}=\begin{cases}<\infty\qquad\textrm{if }v=1/2\\
-3rg^{\prime\prime\prime}(x)\qquad\textrm{if }v=3/2\\
0\qquad\textrm{if }v=1,v>3/2\\
\infty\qquad\textrm{otherwise}\end{cases}

(284)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{5}}{\partial\sigma^{2}\partial x^{3}}\prox_{g}^{\sigma^{2v}r/2}(x)_{|\sigma=0}=\begin{cases}<\infty\qquad\textrm{if }v=1/2\\
-rg^{(4)}(x)\qquad\textrm{if }v=1\\
0\qquad\textrm{if }v>1\\
\infty\qquad\textrm{otherwise}\end{cases}

(285)

Appendix C
Proof of the result for the Laplace distribution

In this section we prove the results in Section
3.2
which give the scaling properties of MY-MALA (and sG-MALA) for the Laplace distribution.
We collect technical results (e.g. moment computations, bounds, etc.) in Appendix
D
.

We recall that
\sigma_{d}^{2}=\ell^{2}/d^{2\alpha}
and
\lambda_{d}=c^{2}/2d^{2\beta}
for some
\alpha,\beta>0
and some constants
c,\ell
independent on
d
.
Thus, we can write
\lambda_{d}
as a function of
\sigma_{d}
,
\lambda_{d}=\sigma_{d}^{2v}r/2
,
where we define
r=c^{2}/\ell^{2v}\geq 0
and
v=\beta/\alpha
. In order to study the scaling limit of MY-MALA with Laplace target,
consider the mapping

b_{d}:\mathbb{R}^{2}\to\mathbb{R}
given by

\displaystyle b_{d}:(x,z)\mapsto z-\frac{\sigma_{d}}{2}\sgn(x)\mathbbm{1}\left\{|x|\geq\sigma_{d}^{2v}r/2\right\}-\frac{1}{\sigma_{d}^{2m-1}r}x\mathbbm{1}\left\{|x|<\sigma_{d}^{2v}r/2\right\},

(286)

which allows us to write the proposal as
Y^{d}_{1,i}=X_{0,i}^{d}+\sigma_{d}b_{d}(X_{0,i}^{d},Z_{1,i}^{d})
, for any
i\in\{1,\dots,d\}
.

We consider also the function
\phi_{d}:\mathbb{R}^{2}\to\mathbb{R}
, given by

\displaystyle\phi_{d}:(x,z)

\displaystyle\mapsto\log\frac{\pi(x+\sigma_{d}b_{d}(x,z))q(x+\sigma_{d}b_{d}(x,z),x)}{\pi(x)q(x,x+\sigma_{d}b_{d}(x,z))}

(287)

\displaystyle=|x|-\left\lvert x+\sigma_{d}b_{d}(x,z)\right\rvert+\frac{z^{2}}{2}

(288)

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{2\sigma_{d}^{2}}\left\{\frac{\sigma_{d}^{2}}{2}\sgn\left[x+\sigma_{d}b_{d}(x,z)\right]\mathbbm{1}\left\{\left|x+\sigma_{d}b_{d}(x,z)\right\rvert\geq\frac{\sigma_{d}^{2v}r}{2}\right\}\right.

(289)

\displaystyle\qquad-\sigma_{d}b_{d}(x,z)

(290)

\displaystyle\qquad+\left.\frac{1}{\sigma_{d}^{2(m-1)}r}\left[x+\sigma_{d}b_{d}(x,z)\right]\mathbbm{1}\left\{\left|x+\sigma_{d}b_{d}(x,z)\right\rvert<\frac{\sigma_{d}^{2v}r}{2}\right\}\right\}^{2}.

(291)

C.1
Proof of Theorem
2

We introduce, for
i\in\{1,\dots,d\}
,
\phi_{d,i}=\phi_{d}(X^{d}_{0,i},Z^{d}_{1,i})

for the sake of conciseness.
This allows us to rewrite
a_{d}(\ell,r)
, defined in (
16
), in
the following way,

a_{d}(\ell,r)=\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\sum_{i=1}^{d}\phi_{d,i}\right)\wedge 1\right].

(292)

Remark 1
.

Under
2
, the families of random variables

(b_{d}(X^{d}_{0,i},Z^{d}_{1,i}))_{i\in\{1,\dots,d\}}
and

(\phi_{d,i})_{i\in\{1,\dots,d\}}
are i.i.d..

Remark 2
.

It is important to see why
Remark

1
and the central limit theorem (CLT)
do not allow us to conclude on the limiting law of the sum of the random variables
(\phi_{d,i})_{i\in\{1,\dots,d\}}
when
d
goes to infinity. To use the CLT, we would need a family of i.i.d. random variables
(T_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}
such that
\mathbb{E}[T_{1}^{2}]<+\infty
. In which case, we would obtain the convergence in distribution of the random variable

\left.\sum_{i=1}^{d}\left(T_{i}-\mathbb{E}\left[T_{1}\right]\right)\middle/\left(\mathbb{E}\left[T_{1}^{2}\right]d\right)^{1/2}\right..

(293)

In our case,
\phi_{d,i}
would play the role of

T_{i}/(\mathbb{E}[T_{1}^{2}]d)^{1/2}
. However, the comparison is flawed because, as one can see in (
287
), there is no easy way to choose a map
f:\mathbb{N}^{*}\to\mathbb{R}
such that
f(d)\phi_{d,i}
no longer depends on
d
. Indeed, the random variable
\phi_{d,i}
depends on
d
in a very intricate way, such that the CLT is not of any help here. This is why we use Lindeberg’s CLT for martingale arrays instead (see
[
45
, Theorem 4, page 543]
).

The proof of Theorem
2
uses the first three moments
of
\phi_{d,1}
, whose computation is postponed to
Appendix
D.1
, and is an application of Lindeberg’s
central limit theorem.

To identify the optimal scaling for the Laplace distribution, we look for those
values of
\alpha
such that
\sum_{i=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}[\phi_{d,i}]
and

\var(\sum_{i=1}^{d}\phi_{d,i})
converge to a finite value. Using

Remark

1
, we have that,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}\left[\phi_{d,i}\right]=d\;\mathbb{E}\left[\phi_{d,1}\right]\;\quad\text{and}\quad\var\left(\sum_{i=1}^{d}\phi_{d,i}\right)=d\var\left(\phi_{d,1}\right).

(294)

Then, using the integrals in Appendix
D.1
, we find that
the only value of
\alpha
for which (
294
) converge to a
finite value with the variance strictly positive is

\alpha=1/3
as confirmed empirically in Appendix
E.2
.

Having identified
\alpha=1/3
, we can then proceed applying Lindeberg’s CLT.

Proof of Theorem
2
.

We start by showing that the acceptance ratio converges to a Gaussian distribution.
Define
\mu_{d}=\mathbb{E}[\phi_{d,1}]
and

\mathcal{F}_{d,i}=\sigma((X_{0,j}^{d},Z^{d}_{1,j}),1\leq j\leq i)
, the natural
filtration for
(X_{0,i}^{d},Z^{d}_{1,i})_{d\in\mathbb{N},1\leq i\leq d}
. The
square-integrable martingale sequence

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{j=1}^{i}W_{d,j},\mathcal{F}_{d,i}\right)_{d\in\mathbb{N}^{*},1\leq i\leq d}

(295)

where
W_{d,i}=\phi_{d,i}-\mu_{d}
,
forms a triangular array, to which we can apply the corresponding CLT (e.g.

[
45
, Theorem 4, page 543]
). In particular, we have that,

\displaystyle\lim_{d\to\infty}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}\left[W_{d,i}^{2}\mid\mathcal{F}_{d,i-1}\right]

\displaystyle=\lim_{d\to\infty}d\var\left(\phi_{d,1}\right)=\frac{2\ell^{3}}{3\sqrt{2\uppi}},

(296)

as shown in Proposition
17
in Appendix
D.1
.
It remains to verify Lindeberg’s condition: for
\varepsilon>0
,

\displaystyle\lim_{d\to\infty}d\mathbb{E}\left[W_{d,1}^{2}\mathbbm{1}\left\{|W_{d,1}|>\varepsilon\right\}\right]=0.

(297)

In order to verify Lindeberg’s condition we verify the stronger Lyapunov condition: there exists

\epsilon>0
such that

\displaystyle\lim_{d\to\infty}d\mathbb{E}\left[W_{d,1}^{2+\epsilon}\right]=0.

(298)

Pick
\epsilon=1
and expand the cube using
\mu_{d}=\mathbb{E}[\phi_{d,i}]
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[W_{d,1}^{3}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\phi_{d,i}^{3}\right]-3\mu_{d}\mathbb{E}\left[\phi_{d,i}^{2}\right]+2\mu_{d}^{3}.

(299)

By Proposition
16
in Appendix
D.1
, we have

\lim_{d\to\infty}d\mu_{d}^{3}=0
,
\lim_{d\to\infty}\mu_{d}=0
,
and, by Proposition
17
in Appendix
D.1
,

\displaystyle\lim_{d\to\infty}d\mathbb{E}\left[\phi_{d,i}^{2}\right]=\frac{2\ell^{3}}{3\sqrt{2\uppi}}.

(300)

Finally, for the remaining term in (
299
) we use
Proposition
18
in Appendix
D.1
to
show that
\lim_{d\to\infty}d\mathbb{E}[\phi_{d,i}^{3}]=0
. The above and the
fact that, by Proposition
16
in
Appendix
D.1
,

\displaystyle\lim_{d\to\infty}d\mu_{d}=-\frac{\ell^{3}}{3\sqrt{2\uppi}},

(301)

show, by Lindeberg’s CLT, that the acceptance ratio converges in law to a normal random variable
\widetilde{Z}
with mean

-\ell^{3}/(3\sqrt{2\uppi})
and
variance
2\ell^{3}/(3\sqrt{2\uppi})
.

To conclude the proof, we apply the continuous mapping theorem to the bounded and continuous function
x\mapsto e^{x}\wedge 1
and obtain

\lim_{d\to\infty}\exp\left(\sum_{i=1}^{d}\phi_{d,i}\right)\wedge 1\overset{\mathrm{d}}{=}e^{\widetilde{Z}}\wedge 1\;\quad\text{and}\quad\lim_{d\to\infty}a_{d}(\ell,r)=\mathbb{E}\left[e^{\widetilde{Z}}\wedge 1\right],

(302)

where the limit does not depend on
r
. Defining
a^{\mathrm{L}}(\ell)=\lim_{d\to\infty}a_{d}(\ell,r)
and
using
[
36
, Proposition 2.4]
, we have the result.

∎

C.2
Proof of Proposition
1

We are interested in the law
\nu_{d}
of the linear
interpolant
(L_{t}^{d})_{t\geq 0}
, defined in (
15
),
of the first component of the chain
(X^{d}_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
. Let us recall the definition of the chain:
assumption
2
gives the initial distribution
\pi_{d}
,
then, for any
k\in\mathbb{N}
, the proposal
Y^{d}_{k+1}=(Y^{d}_{k+1,i})_{1\leq i\leq d}
is defined in (
28
) with
\sigma_{d}^{2}=\ell^{2}/d^{2\alpha}
,
\lambda_{d}=\sigma_{d}^{2v}r/2
with
\alpha=1/3
and
v\geq 1
. The proposal (
28
) can be written as

Y^{d}_{k+1,i}=X^{d}_{k,i}+\sigma_{d}b_{d}(X^{d}_{k,i},Z^{d}_{k+1,i}),

(303)

for any
i\in\{1,\dots,d\}
, where
b_{d}
is defined in (
286
) and
r=c^{2}/\ell^{2v}
.
We further define the acceptance event
\mathsf{A}_{k+1}^{d}=\left\{\mathrm{b}_{k+1}^{d}=1\right\}
where
\mathrm{b}_{k+1}^{d}
is as in (
7
).

We can now expand the expression of the linear interpolant
L_{t}^{d}
using (
7
), (
15
) and the definition of

\mathsf{A}_{k+1}^{d}
,

\displaystyle L_{t}^{d}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}X^{d}_{\lfloor d^{2\alpha}t\rfloor,1}+(d^{2\alpha}t-\lfloor d^{2\alpha}t\rfloor)\left[\sigma_{d}Z^{d}_{\lceil d^{2\alpha}t\rceil,1}-\frac{\sigma^{2}_{d}}{2}\sgn(X^{d}_{\lfloor d^{2\alpha}t\rfloor,1})\right]\mathbbm{1}_{\mathsf{A}^{d}_{\lceil d^{2\alpha}t\rceil}}\\
\qquad\qquad\textrm{if }|X^{d}_{\lfloor d^{2\alpha}t\rfloor,1}|\geq\frac{\sigma^{2v}_{d}r}{2}\\
X^{d}_{\lfloor d^{2\alpha}t\rfloor,1}+(d^{2\alpha}t-\lfloor d^{2\alpha}t\rfloor)\left[\sigma_{d}Z^{d}_{\lceil d^{2\alpha}t\rceil,1}-\frac{1}{\sigma_{d}^{2(m-1)}r}X^{d}_{\lfloor d^{2\alpha}t\rfloor,1}\right]\mathbbm{1}_{\mathsf{A}^{d}_{\lceil d^{2\alpha}t\rceil}}\\
\qquad\qquad\textrm{otherwise}\end{cases},

(304)

or, equivalently,

\displaystyle L_{t}^{d}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}X^{d}_{\lceil d^{2\alpha}t\rceil,1}-(\lceil d^{2\alpha}t\rceil-d^{2\alpha}t)\left[\sigma_{d}Z^{d}_{\lceil d^{2\alpha}t\rceil,1}-\frac{\sigma^{2}_{d}}{2}\sgn(X^{d}_{\lfloor d^{2\alpha}t\rfloor,1})\right]\mathbbm{1}_{\mathsf{A}^{d}_{\lceil d^{2\alpha}t\rceil}}\\
\qquad\qquad\textrm{if }|X^{d}_{\lfloor d^{2\alpha}t\rfloor,1}|\geq\frac{\sigma^{2v}_{d}r}{2}\\
X^{d}_{\lceil d^{2\alpha}t\rceil,1}-(\lceil d^{2\alpha}t\rceil-d^{2\alpha}t)\left[\sigma_{d}Z^{d}_{\lceil d^{2\alpha}t\rceil,1}-\frac{1}{\sigma_{d}^{2(m-1)}r}X^{d}_{\lfloor d^{2\alpha}t\rfloor,1}\right]\mathbbm{1}_{\mathsf{A}^{d}_{\lceil d^{2\alpha}t\rceil}}\\
\qquad\qquad\textrm{otherwise}\end{cases}.

(305)

In order to prove Proposition
1
, we consider Kolmogorov’s
criterion for tightness (see
[
25
, Theorem 23.7]
): the sequence
(\nu_{d})_{d\geq 1}
is tight if the sequence
(L^{d}_{0})_{d\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}
is tight, and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[(L_{t}^{d}-L_{s}^{d})^{4}\right]\leq\gamma(t)(t-s)^{2},

(306)

for some non-decreasing positive function
\gamma
, all
0\leq s\leq t
and
all
d\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
. The condition on
(L^{d}_{0})_{d\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}
is straightforward to check, since by
2
the distribution of
L^{d}_{0}=X^{d}_{0,1}
is
\pi^{\mathrm{L}}
for all
d\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
.
Before proceeding with the proof of the proposition we briefly recall the following Hölder’s inequality (which holds for any
p\geq 1
and any
a_{i}\geq 0
)

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}\right)^{p}\leq n^{p-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(a_{i})^{p},

(307)

of which we will make frequent use.

Proof of Proposition
1
.

Consider
\mathbb{E}\left[(L_{t}^{d}-L_{s}^{d})^{4}\right]
with
L_{t}^{d},L_{s}^{d}
as in (
304
).

If
\lfloor d^{2\alpha}s\rfloor=\lfloor d^{2\alpha}t\rfloor
(which implies
\lceil d^{2\alpha}s\rceil=\lceil d^{2\alpha}t\rceil
) and
|X^{d}_{\lfloor d^{2\alpha}t\rfloor,1}|=|X^{d}_{\lfloor d^{2\alpha}s\rfloor,1}|\geq\sigma_{d}^{2v}r/2
, we have

\displaystyle L_{t}^{d}-L_{s}^{d}

\displaystyle=(d^{2\alpha}t-d^{2\alpha}s)\left[\sigma_{d}Z^{d}_{\lceil d^{2\alpha}t\rceil,1}-\frac{\sigma^{2}_{d}}{2}\sgn(X^{d}_{\lfloor d^{2\alpha}t\rfloor,1})\right]\mathbbm{1}_{\mathsf{A}^{d}_{\lceil d^{2\alpha}t\rceil}}.

(308)

Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[(L_{t}^{d}-L_{s}^{d})^{4}\right]

\displaystyle=(d^{2\alpha}t-d^{2\alpha}s)^{4}\mathbb{E}\left[\left[\sigma_{d}Z^{d}_{\lceil d^{2\alpha}t\rceil,1}-\frac{\sigma^{2}_{d}}{2}\sgn(X^{d}_{\lfloor d^{2\alpha}t\rfloor,1})\right]\mathbbm{1}_{\mathsf{A}^{d}_{\lceil d^{2\alpha}t\rceil}}\right].

(309)

By bounding the indicator function by one and using (
307
) we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[(L_{t}^{d}-L_{s}^{d})^{4}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2^{3}(d^{2\alpha}t-d^{2\alpha}s)^{4}\mathbb{E}\left[\sigma_{d}^{4}(Z^{d}_{\lceil d^{2\alpha}t\rceil,1})^{4}+\frac{\sigma^{8}_{d}}{2^{4}}\sgn(X^{d}_{\lfloor d^{2\alpha}t\rfloor,1})^{4}\right]

(310)

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{(d^{2\alpha}t-d^{2\alpha}s)^{4}}{(d^{2\alpha})^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[(Z^{d}_{\lceil d^{2\alpha}t\rceil,1})^{4}+\frac{\sigma^{2}_{d}}{2^{4}}\right]

(311)

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{(d^{2\alpha}t-d^{2\alpha}s)^{4}}{(d^{2\alpha})^{2}}.

(312)

where we used the boundedness of the
\sgn
function and the of the moments of normal distributions and we incorporated all constants in
C
.
Moreover, using
\lfloor d^{2\alpha}s\rfloor=\lfloor d^{2\alpha}t\rfloor
, we have
(d^{2\alpha}t-d^{2\alpha}s)^{4}\leq(d^{2\alpha}t-d^{2\alpha}s)^{2}
. Hence,
